Decode: 1747 Abdali founded the Afghan state after Nadir Shah's death; 1756 he invaded and installed his eleven-year-old son Timur Shah; 1758 Raghunath Rao 73-74 removed Timur from Lahore (peak of Maratha power); 1761 Third Battle of Panipat.

Why it sticks: four beats, four dates.

Memory hook

Son shoved out, Father storms in.

The immediate cause of Panipat III was Timur Shah's expulsion from Lahore (March 1758), not Maratha growth in general.

Decode: answer (a) = avenge the Marathas' expulsion of his viceroy Timur Shah from Lahore. Wrong options: (b) fast Maratha expansion (background only), (c) Mughal tribute dispute (none), (d) "Sultan of Punjab" refusing passage (none).

Why it sticks: Lahore, son, revenge: a family quarrel first, geopolitics second.

10.2 Why Abdali won: six factors

Memory hook

A Lion Tamer Found Lazy Fighters.

A-L-T-F-L-F: the six reasons Abdali won.

Decode: Alliance (Awadh encircled and joined; Rohilkhand and Farrukhabad backed Abdali; Sikhs, Rajputs, Jats neutral; 60,000 versus 50,000); Logistics (supply cut between Panipat and Delhi); Terrain (open plains, no hills for guerrilla tactics); Firepower (horse-mounted swivel guns, steadier and better aimed because attached to the seat); Leadership (Abdali and Najib-ud-Daula 57-70); Factionalism (Raghuji Bhonsle of Nagpur refused to fight).

Why it sticks: the teacher files Logistics and Terrain under TACTICS.

10.3 Impact of the defeat

Memory hook

Two bulls fight, the shopkeeper takes the field.

The Marathas and Abdali lock horns at Panipat; neither rules India; the EIC walks in.

Decode: Panipat III did not decide who would rule India but that neither the Marathas nor Abdali would (power vacuum). Marathas lost Sadasiv Rao Bhau and Vishwas Rao, Nana Saheb died within weeks, about 50,000 lost; Abdali had heavy losses and a revolt among his generals. Next Peshwas: Madhav Rao 61-72, Narayan Rao 72-73, infant Madhav Rao II 74-95. The vacuum was filled by the EIC.

Why it sticks: both bulls lose, the shopkeeper gains.

11 · After Panipat: decline with moments of strength (1761-1819, about 60 years)

Memory hook

CREM, with Guntur on top later.

Chicacole, Rajmandri, Ellore, MustafaNagar = C-R-E-M; Guntur (MurtuzaNagar) is added later.

Decode: 1766 Treaty of Hyderabad: the Northern Sarkars go to the EIC for Rs 7 lakh a year plus protection against the Marathas and Mysore; four districts at first (C-R-E-M); Guntur was not transferred in 1766.

Why it sticks: four letters first, the fifth comes later.

Memory hook

After Panipat: a year-rhyme.

'65 Mysore pays, '66 Sarkars sold,

'71 Mahadji takes Delhi bold,

'75 to '82 the EIC loses a war, '82 Salbai seals the score,

'84 Campoo drills, '95 Kharda thrills,

'98 Hyderabad signs the first Subsidiary line,

'03 Delhi goes, '17-'19 Svarajya's last sign.

Decode: 1765 Marathas defeat Mysore; 1766 Treaty of Hyderabad (Northern Sarkars); 1771 Mahadji Scindia 68-94 recaptures Delhi; 1775-82 First Anglo-Maratha War; 1782 Treaty of Salbai (Maratha-EIC alliance); 1784 de Boigne's Fauj-i-Hind (Campoo); 1795 Battle of Kharda (Marathas beat Hyderabad); 1798 Hyderabad first Subsidiary Alliance; 1803 EIC captures Delhi; 1817-19 Svarajya annexed.

Why it sticks: rhyme pairs keep the order; read it out loud once.

Memory hook

Win, lose Delhi, lose all.

The three Anglo-Maratha Wars: the Marathas win the first, the EIC takes Delhi in the second, the third ends Svarajya.

Decode: First AMW 1775-82 (the only war of that period the EIC lost); Second AMW 1803-05 (EIC captured Delhi in 1803); Third AMW 1817-19 (Svarajya annexed, last Peshwa Baji Rao II 96-18 ended).

Why it sticks: W-L-L, one win then two losses.

Memory hook

Campoo = a camp with everything: I-C-A.

Own Infantry, own Cavalry, own Artillery: a mini-army in every regiment.

Decode: 1784 Mahadji Shinde (Scindia) hired Benoit de Boigne to raise the Fauj-i-Hind (the Campoo system); each Campoo self-sufficient (own infantry, cavalry, artillery, transport, supplies, weapons); benefits: speed, and a lower chance of a coup by a commander; Holkar hired Dudrenec; later link: the Risala system of Haider Ali 61-82.

Why it sticks: Campoo sounds like "camp": a self-contained camp.

Memory hook

Eight treaties: Pandit Wants Bananas, But Sings Beautiful Hindi Songs.

P-W-B-B-S-B-H-S in year order.

Decode: Purandhar 1665; Warna 1731; Bhopal 1738; Bassein 1739; Sangola 1750; Bhalke 1751; Hyderabad 1766; Salbai 1782. Treaties are named after the place where signed.

Why it sticks: the years bunch as 31-38-39, then 50-51, then 66 and 82.

12 · Why couldn't the Marathas establish all-India rule?

Memory hook

The Wise Sardars Stayed Separate.

T-W-S-S-S: why there was no all-India Maratha rule. Control tick, Rule cross.

Decode: Tribute not annexation; Weak centre, strong Sardars; Sangola 1750; Svarajya itself not centralised (local assemblies of Vatan-holders); Samrajya had no government machinery on the ground in many areas.

Why it sticks: the Marathas could collect (control) but not govern (rule): extracting tribute is not ruling.

Mains 2014: why so many battles at Panipat?

Memory hook

The gate has a WWF crew.

Not the town but the gateway to Delhi from the Khyber, helped by Weather, Warriors, Fields, Farms.

Decode: gateway to Delhi for powers from Central Asia or Afghanistan via the Khyber Pass (Panipat, Karnal, Kurukshetra, Thanesar); supporting factors: weather for long campaigns, many warrior communities for recruitment, vast open fields, agricultural productivity plus proximity to Delhi for supply.

Why it sticks: W-W-F-F, and "not the town, the gate".

Memory hook

Delhi's doorbell rang six times.

Thanesar twice, Panipat thrice, Karnal once.

Decode: Thanesar 1014 (Mahmud of Ghazni), Thanesar 1191 (Muhammad Ghori), Panipat I 1526 (Delhi Sultanate shaken), Panipat II 1556 (Hemu's rule ended), Karnal 1739 (Mughals), Panipat III 1761 (Marathas). 2 + 3 + 1 = 6.

Why it sticks: the pairs 1526/1556 and 1739/1761 share their first two digits (15 and 17).

Memory hooks: chapter in one breath

Memory hook

One breath: from the boy at 16 to Svarajya's end.

Shivaji 74-80 takes a fort at 16 (1646), tiger-claws Afzal in '59, bows at Purandhar in '65, and crowns himself at Raigarh in '74.

Sambhaji 80-89 is killed, Rajaram 89-00 dies, Tarabai rules as regent; Shahu 19-49 is freed in '07, and the civil war runs 07-19 (7 + 12).

In 1719 Balaji Vishwanath 13-20 trades with the Sayyids (A-C-S), and the decade 27-39 follows: Gujarat 60%, Malwa, Delhi plundered '37, Bhopal '38, Bassein '39.

Sangola 1750 hands every power to the Peshwa; Bhalke '51, Ahmedabad '52, Alamgir '54.

In '58 Timur is shooed from Lahore, so Abdali comes: Panipat 1761, six reasons (A-L-T-F-L-F), and neither side rules; the EIC takes the field.

Then '82 Salbai, '84 Campoo, '98 the first Subsidiary Alliance, 1803 Delhi lost, and 1817-19 Svarajya annexed.

Chapter 5: The Jat Kingdom & the Afghan Kingdoms (23)open chapter

1 · The middle path between king and rebel

Memory hook

The middle-path bargain: MAT and DoWN.

The rebel lays the MAT; the emperor gives the DoWN pillow; both sleep in peace.

Decode: Rebel gives = Mansabdar status (and accepts the Mughal as emperor), Army (maintains it, helps militarily), Tribute to Delhi. Emperor gives = Dynastic governorship, Watan Jagir, Non-interference.

Why it sticks: MAT is what the rebel offers, DWN is what he gets back; the empire does not shrink.

Memory hook

Sorting the regional powers: BAH! / Ma-Ro-Fa / Raj-My.

Successors shout "BAH!"; Rebels chant "Ma-Ro-Fa"; the Autonomous never joined the club.

Decode: SS = Bengal, Awadh, Hyderabad (BAH). RS = Marathas, Rohilkhand, Farrukhabad (plus the Sikhs). AAS = Rajputs and Mysore (plus Travancore).

Why it sticks: the Sikhs (RS) and Travancore (AAS) are the two "not legally in the Mughal empire" odd ones; the Sikhs never accepted Mughal symbolic sovereignty, except once in 1707.

2 · Framework: three more Rebel States

Memory hook

Three rebel states, three dates: 14, 21, 37-40.

F comes before R, in the alphabet and in time: Farrukhabad first, Rohilkhand next.

Decode: Farrukhabad = est. 1714 (Bangash Pathans). Rohilkhand = process began 1721, completed 1737-40 (Rohilla Afghans, around Bareilly). The third, the Jat Kingdom of Bharatpur, sits in East Rajasthan south of Delhi.

Why it sticks: the numbers climb 14, 21, then 37-40, just like the alphabet F, R.

3.2 How the resistance developed

Memory hook

The Jat chain: Great Rebels Created Bharatpur's Stronghold.

G-R-C-B-S = Gokla, Rajaram 89-00, Churaman 95-21, Badan Singh, Surajmal 56-63.

Decode: Gokla 1669-70 (first organised revolt) → Rajaram 89-00 (guerrilla warfare) → Churaman 95-21 (Created the kingdom, Bharatpur) → Badan Singh 1722-55 (Bridge, consolidates) → Surajmal 56-63 (Summit, most powerful). Hostility began with Jahangir 05-27 over LR and local autonomy.

Why it sticks: only the two teacher-marked "imp" names carry a job word: C = Created, S = Summit.

Memory hook

Lohagarh = Loha + garh.

Loha (iron) + garh (fort) = the Iron Fort, Surajmal 56-63's pride.

Decode: Lohagarh Fort, Bharatpur, built by Maharaja Surajmal 56-63; never conquered, even by the British.

Why it sticks: the name itself is the fact.

3.3 Surajmal 56-63: the peak

Memory hook

Jats at Panipat 1761: "No to the plan, yes to the ration."

Surajmal 56-63 said no to the plan, but his carts still rolled.

Decode: alliance failed over war strategy; Surajmal 56-63 wanted guerrilla warfare, Sadasiv Rao Bhau disagreed; the Jats still sent essential supplies. Contrast: Rohillas and Bangash joined Abdali, the Jats did not.

Why it sticks: guerrilla (his plan) versus supplies (his help), two separate facts in one line.

Memory hook

Surajmal 56-63's three reforms: E-R-K.

Surajmal 56-63 ran his State like an ERK: Europeans in, Revenue copied, Kin out.

Decode: E = recruited European soldiers; R = Mughal-style revenue system; K = removed relatives (kin) from court posts to cut factionalism. Territory: Delhi to Agra, Chambal to Ganga (D-A, C-G).

Why it sticks: Reform 1 matches Mahadji Scindia 68-94's Campoo (modern troops); Reform 3 is the contrast with R vs R infighting.

3.4 Was the Jat Kingdom a better deal for the peasant?

Memory hook

Jat State: peasant revolt, Zamindar rule.

Born in the field, ruled from the haveli: "Kisan ki kranti, zamindar ka raj."

Decode: result of peasant revolts, but no peasant utopia: land revenue stayed high, real power with Zamindars not ryots, same extractive structure it rose against; collapse after Surajmal 56-63 died in 1763.

Why it sticks: the revolt and the rule have different owners.

4.2 Was there ever a moment when Afghans actually ruled Delhi?

Memory hook

Sher Shah 40-45's life in one climb: Sur to Sasaram to Sirhind.

Farid from Sasaram ruled Delhi for five years (40 to 45); Humayun 30-40 returned at the double-five (55).

Decode: Sur clan (Pashtun) migrates in the Lodi period → grandfather's jagir at Sasaram (Bihar) → Farid Khan born c.1486 → serves under Mughals after Babur 26-30 beats the Lodis in 1526 and studies their administration → 1540 Kanauj: beats Humayun 30-40, seizes Delhi → dies 1545 → Suri Interregnum 1540-55 → 1555 Sirhind, Humayun 30-40 retakes Delhi (dies 1556).

Why it sticks: S-S-S (Sur, Sasaram, Sirhind) bookends the story; 40, 45, 55 climb by 5 and 10.

Memory hook

Afghan markers on the board: S-F-R-A.

Sher Found Rohilla Allies.

Decode: Suris 1540-55 → Farrukhabad 1714 → Rohilkhand 1737-40 → Abdali 1748-61.

Why it sticks: the order is also chronological; each letter is one Afghan marker on the teacher's board timeline.

4.3 What Sher Shah 40-45 Suri 40-45 achieved in five years

Memory hook

Sher Shah 40-45's five reforms: SHE-R-R.

Sher Shah 40-45 "SHE-roars" twice at the end: R, R.

Decode: S = Suba-Sarkar-Pargana hierarchy (Centre, Province, District, Sub-district; Akbar 56-05 adopted it); H = Highway, the Grand Trunk Road (upgraded Uttarapath, Bengal to North-West Frontier); E = Express relay post, Dak Chowki ("like a relay race"); R = Rupiya, standard silver coin (trust plus central authority); R = Revenue, surveys and fair demand direct with the peasant.

Why it sticks: five letters, five reforms, and he had only five years (1540-45).

Memory hook

Patta and Qubuliat: head and tail.

Flip the coin: Patta is the Peasant's page; the other tail belongs to the government.

Decode: one document, two copies. Patta = with the ryot (P for Patta, P for Peasant); Qubuliat = with the government.

Why it sticks: the teacher's own "head and tail" image plus one shared initial P.

6.1 How did a Bangash Pathan warlord found a kingdom near Agra?

Memory hook

Farrukhabad's three rulers: Makes, Quits, Avenges.

Md Khan Makes it, Qaim Khan Quits (killed), Ahmad Khan Avenges.

Decode: Md Khan Bangash 1714-43 makes Farrukhabad (12,000 troops for Farrukhsiyar 13-19, rank of Nawab, revenue near the Ganga) → Qaim Khan 1743-49 quits: 1749 Safdar Jung 39-54 engineers his death, Farrukhabad annexed to Awadh and plundered → Ahmad Khan retakes it in 1750.

Why it sticks: M-Q-A also runs in time order, with the dates 14, 43, 49, 50.

Memory hook

J then F: the killer was killed.

Jahandar Shah 12-13 struck first, Farrukhsiyar 13-19 struck back; J before F, like the alphabet.

Decode: Jahandar Shah 12-13 seized power by killing a relative (the teacher's board: his brother; the handout: Farrukhsiyar 13-19's father); Farrukhsiyar 13-19 then removed Jahandar Shah 12-13. Following the chapter's own ruling, both versions are in the class material; what to write is Mughal succession violence (R vs R).

Why it sticks: remember the pattern "killer, then killed", not the victim's relationship.

Memory hook

Mohammad-abad (me-town) became Farrukh-abad (your-town).

"My town" annoyed the king; "Your town" calmed him.

Decode: Md Khan Bangash first founded Mohammadabad after himself, which annoyed Farrukhsiyar 13-19; Farrukhabad ("city of Farrukh") was the diplomatic correction: sovereignty in name, autonomy on the ground.

Why it sticks: one word swap, the name tells the politics.

6.2 Ahmad Khan Bangash 50-71, 1750 onwards

Memory hook

Ahmad Khan's see-saw: Win, Lose, Deal, Win, Lose.

W-L-D-W-L: five beats, five dates.

Decode: 1750 Win (retakes Farrukhabad) → 1751-52 Lose (Marathas under Holkar and Scindia plus Jats under Surajmal 56-63, at Awadh's request) → 1752 Deal (treaty: returns, half of Farrukhabad to Holkar and Scindia) → 1761 Win (fights beside Abdali 47-72 at Panipat, recovers full dominions) → 1802 Lose (EIC annexes). Chapter's ruling: the short code for him is inconsistent (50-71 or 50-82), so anchor on 1750 and 1802.

Why it sticks: the story alternates, so you cannot lose track of the turn.

7.1 How Afghan mercenaries became rulers

Memory hook

Katehr to Rohilkhand: Roh, Rohilla, Rohil-khand.

Roh is home; Rohilla is the man from Roh; Rohilkhand is his khand (land).

Decode: Roh = the Afghan homeland; Rohillas = the hired Afghan mercenaries of Katehr (around Bareilly and Moradabad, western UP); Katehr was renamed Rohilkhand in 1740.

Why it sticks: the word builds itself: Roh + illa + khand.

7.4 How the Mughals responded

Memory hook

Ali Md Khan's three A's: Absorb, Absent, Appointed.

21, 39, 40: Absorb the land, go Absent from Delhi's treasury, get Appointed by the emperor.

Decode: Absorb = 1721, after Daud Khan's death his adopted son takes over and spends two decades absorbing lands from the Zamindars; Absent = 1739, Nadir Shah paralyses Delhi, so he stops paying taxes; Appointed = 1740, Emperor Md Shah (19-48) grants the Subadar title, Katehr renamed Rohilkhand.

Why it sticks: the Mughals' rule was "co-opt what you cannot defeat": the title came only after the defiance.

7.5 How Rohilkhand ended

Memory hook

Najib-ud-Daula 57-70's slide: 61, 70, 71, 74.

Najib-ud-Daula 57-70 backed Abdali 47-72 (61), died (70), Delhi slipped (71), Rohilkhand fell (74), but Rampur survived.

Decode: 1761 Najib-ud-Daula 57-70 backs Abdali at Panipat; 1761-71 Delhi under Rohilla influence → 1770 Najib dies → 1771 Mahadji Scindia 68-94 retakes Delhi, Shah Alam II 60-06 returns → 1774 Awadh plus EIC annex most of Rohilkhand (Treaty of Allahabad 1765 made them allies); the remainder is Rampur (capital Bareilly), under the EIC from 1801.

Why it sticks: 70 and 71 are neighbours, then 74 closes the book, with the 1765 treaty as the old pact behind it.

8 · Why Abdali came: the immediate trigger

Memory hook

Prelims 2010: Abdali 47-72 came for his son.

Not tribute, not safe passage, not Maratha size: it was the Timur Tears of March 1758.

Decode: Raghunath Rao 73-74 drove Timur Shah (Abdali's son, Governor of Punjab and Kashmir) out of Lahore in March 1758; options (b) Maratha expansion, (c) tribute refusal and (d) safe passage are all wrong; answer (a), the expulsion.

Why it sticks: the trap options are about politics, the real reason is about a family member.

Memory hook

Punjab under the Marathas: Adina pays, Sabaji guards.

A before S: Adina Beg buys the chair, Sabaji stands at the gate.

Decode: Adina Beg Khan = Governor of Punjab for Rs 75 lakh a year (the auctioned office); on his death Sabaji Scindia = in charge, with garrisons at Attock and Peshawar on the Afghan border. "Attock se Cuttack" = Maratha reach (Cuttack = Orissa).

Why it sticks: 75 lakh to buy the post; Attock is the western end of the phrase.

9 · Synthesis: three Rebel States, one pattern

Memory hook

Three states, three directions, one prop.

Jats come Up from the soil, Farrukhabad comes Down from the court, Rohilkhand grows from the Roots; all stand on a prop.

Decode: Jats = agrarian base upward (Surajmal 56-63); Farrukhabad = Mughal court politics downward (12,000 troops, Nawab title); Rohilkhand = ground up over two decades (mercenaries, 1739). Prop = Abdali for the Afghan kingdoms, Maratha tactical backing for Surajmal 56-63; remove it and each collapses (1761 Abdali leaves; 1763 Surajmal 56-63 dies).

Why it sticks: Up, Down, Roots, then "remove the prop, roof falls".

Memory hooks: chapter in one breath

Memory hook

The Rebel States, one story from soil to Panipat.

A rebel lays the MAT, the emperor gives the DoWN: that is the middle path.

Soil first: Gokla, Rajaram 89-00, Churaman 95-21 founds Bharatpur, Surajmal 56-63 reaches the summit and dies in 1763.

Then the Afghans: Sher Shah 40-45 beats Humayun 30-40 at Kanauj (1540), Humayun 30-40 returns at Sirhind (1555).

Court next: Md Khan Makes Farrukhabad (1714), Qaim Quits (1749), Ahmad Avenges (1750); EIC ends it in 1802.

Ground-up: Ali Absorbs (1721), goes Absent (1739), is Appointed (1740); Rohilkhand falls in 1774, Rampur remains.

Finally the Marathas expel Timur Shah in March 1758, Abdali 47-72 comes for his son, and the props fall away.

Decode: MAT/DoWN = the bargain; Jat chain G-R-C-B-S; SHE-R-R reforms; M-Q-A rulers; A-A-A Ali; Timur = trigger for Panipat 1761.

Why it sticks: one walk from soil (Jats) to court (Farrukhabad) to ground (Rohilkhand) to Abdali 47-72.

Chapter 6: Punjab & the Sikh State (24)open chapter

Exam lens: priority and PYQs

Memory hook

Chapter 6 exam count: 0 Prelims, 2 Mains.

Zero in Prelims, two in Mains: 14 for Panipat, 17 for the fragmented polity.

Decode: no mapped Prelims question (1995-2025); Mains 2014 = why so many empire-shaking battles at Panipat; Mains 2017 = the spectre of a fragmented polity (Misl confederacy is your Punjab example; the State PCS version says "by 1715 Punjab").

Why it sticks: "0, 2" then the years 14 and 17 in order.

0 · The teacher's opening boards

Memory hook

Five Ks on one hand: Ka-Ki-Ka at the end.

Count the fingers: 1 Kesh (hair), 2 Kangha (comb), 3 Kada, 4 Kirpan (dagger), 5 Kachera (lower wear).

Decode: the teacher's board order is Kesh, Kangha, Kada, Kirpan, Kachera; the board gives no gloss for Kada.

Why it sticks: first two are about the head (hair, comb); the last three sound Ka-Ki-Ka, with Kada as the one without a gloss.

1 · How did Sikhism begin, and what were its core beliefs?

Memory hook

Nanak's four principles: ODRH, "One Direct Road Home".

Walk One Direct Road Home.

Decode: One supreme formless God; Direct access to God without rituals or priests; Rejection of the caste system; Honest living and selfless service. Founder Guru Nanak Dev (1469-1539), contemporary of Babur 26-30; rose from 1500 onwards.

Why it sticks: the road image gives the order of the four ideas.

Memory hook

Udasis: the three-zero pair.

4 journeys, 30 years, 30,000 km: "four trips, thirty twice."

Decode: 4 Udasis; 30 years; about 30,000 km across India, South Asia, Tibet and Arabia. Ending: settled at Kartarpur (present-day Pakistan) and set up the Langar, the free communal kitchen for equality.

Why it sticks: 30 and 30,000 are the same digits, so you only memorise one number plus the 4.

2 · When did conflict begin between the Sikhs and the Mughal State?

Memory hook

Two flashpoints: 5th in 06, 9th in 75.

Five-oh-six, nine-seven-five: Jahangir 05-27's Guru is the 5th, Aurangzeb 58-07's Guru is the 9th.

Decode: 1606, 5th Guru Arjan Dev executed by Jahangir 05-27 (first flashpoint; a peaceful movement turns martial). 1675, 9th Guru Teg Bahadur executed in Delhi by Aurangzeb 58-07 (second flashpoint).

Why it sticks: the Guru's number matches the order of the rulers, early ruler with the earlier Guru.

Memory hook

Anandpur: W of Shimla, S of Chamba.

W-Shimla, S-Chamba: the compass letter comes before the town.

Decode: Anandpur lies west of Shimla and south of Chamba, in the Shivalik foothills, an area under Shivalik hill chiefs who were Mughal allies; the Sikh demand for a State here started the tension.

Why it sticks: "W then S" is the order of the two clues in the sentence.

3 · The Khalsa (1699)

Memory hook

Guru numbers 5, 9, 10 = years 06, 75, 99.

Fifth in 1606, ninth in 1675, tenth in 1699: "6, 75, 99."

Decode: 5th Guru Arjan Dev, 1606, executed by Jahangir 05-27; 9th Guru Teg Bahadur, 1675, executed by Aurangzeb 58-07; 10th Guru Gobind Singh 76-08, 1699, founds the Khalsa at Anandpur (a military body like NSG/STF, to defend the Panth and struggle for a Sikh State).

Why it sticks: the last two digits rise 06, 75, 99; the 10th Guru answers the two executions with an army.

4 · December 1704 to October 1708: the Sahibzadas and the Guru's death

Memory hook

Four Sahibzadas: AJ fought, ZF fell.

Ages fall 18, 14, 9, 6 as the names go A, J, Z, F.

Decode: Dec 1704, Battle of Chamkaur: Ajit Singh (18) and Jujhar Singh (14) died in battle. Same month Zorawar Singh (9) and Fateh Singh (6) were buried alive in a wall on the orders of Wazir Khan, Mughal Faujdar of Sirhind (a Faujdar is like a Superintendent of Police). 26 December = Veer Bal Divas since 2022.

Why it sticks: elders 2 in battle, youngers 2 in the wall; the two pairs are 18-14 and 9-6.

Memory hook

1707 promise, 1708 plot.

Seven: a Promise. Eight: a Plot. And the Pledge is never kept.

Decode: 1707 Bahadur Shah (pacifist policy after Aurangzeb 58-07's death) gives Guru Gobind Singh 76-08 a high Mansab and promises Anandpur; 7 Oct 1708 Wazir Khan's assassins strike at Abchal Nagar Sahib, Nanded; the Guru kills them but dies of his injuries aged 42. Wazir Khan is not punished; Anandpur is not given.

Why it sticks: P-P-P: Promise, Plot, Pledge broken, one letter, one year each.

5 · Why the Guru declared the Guru Granth Sahib the final Guru

Memory hook

Jat = Jang, Khatri = Khazana: why the Book became Guru.

Jats fought, Khatris funded; the Gurus were Khatris, so the Guru gave the crown to a Book.

Decode: Jats dominated the Khalsa (the army); Khatris were traders and moneylenders, and all ten Gurus were Khatris. Guru Gobind Singh 76-08 vested authority in the Guru Granth Sahib Ji (installed at the Akal Takht, where Hukumnamas were issued) to end the rivalry for good. Exception: 19th-century Nirankaris and Namdharis believe in a living Guru.

Why it sticks: J-J (Jat, Jang) versus K-K (Khatri, Khazana); a Book cannot be a Jat or a Khatri.

6 · Banda Bahadur 08-15: the first Sikh State, and why it collapsed

Memory hook

Banda Bahadur 08-15's State: Ravi to Jamuna, COS.

Banda Bahadur 08-15's State kept the COS: Coins, Officials, Seal.

Decode: From 1708 Banda Bahadur 08-15 carried on; 10 May 1710 Chappar Chiri, kills Wazir Khan, conquers Sirhind; State between the Ravi and the Jamuna with its own Coins, Officials and Seal (full independence). Farrukhsiyar 13-19 sends Abdus Samad Khan as Governor of Lahore; 1715 defeated, 1716 executed. Chapter ruling on territory: the handout says Ravi to Jamuna (the answer-writing wording); the teacher's ink says "in and around Patiala".

Why it sticks: COS = the three marks of a real State.

Memory hook

Wazir Khan's circle: 04, 08, 10.

Oh-four he walled the boys; oh-eight he sent the knives; ten, the circle closed.

Decode: Dec 1704 buries the two younger Sahibzadas alive; 7 Oct 1708 arranges assassins against Guru Gobind Singh 76-08; 10 May 1710 killed by Banda Bahadur 08-15 at Chappar Chiri, in revenge.

Why it sticks: 04, 08, 10 steps by 4 then 2, and the third event is the revenge.

Memory hook

Why Banda Bahadur 08-15 fell: the K-D-J DJ.

The K-D-J DJ changed the tune three times and the crowd walked off the floor.

Decode: K = Khatri traders turned (constant war hurt trade; commerce wants a decisive winner); D = Divide and rule: Jahandar Shah 12-13 brought Ijaradari to Punjab to co-opt Khatris, and both he and Farrukhsiyar 13-19 offered them Mansabs; J = Jat zamindars switched, e.g. Churaman 95-21 went over after the Mughals recognised his Jat Kingdom. Also: Banda Bahadur 08-15 was not a Guru.

Why it sticks: three letters, three reasons, in the chapter's own order (1, 2, 3).

7 · What kept the Sikh State alive: the Misl confederacy

Memory hook

Misls: a dozen eggs in the Dal Khalsa basket.

12 Misls in one basket; the teacher's 40-60 is only a rough sketch.

Decode: Misls = kinship clans; united in wars (esp. against Afghan invasions), otherwise independent; conquered land shared by military and monetary contribution (even the lowest soldier got a share). By 1770: 12 major Misls, with the Dal Khalsa (assembly of Misl chiefs) at the centre. Chapter ruling: the handout's formal count is 12 by 1770; the board's "roughly 40-60" is the sketch. Manupur 1748: Dal Khalsa meets on Abdali 47-72's invasion, Abdali 47-72 defeated. Same shape as the Maratha Confederacy under the Peshwas.

Why it sticks: 12 = a dozen, a basket with a weak centre.

Memory hook

Fragmented polity: Four Ills Strike Weak centres.

F-I-S-W: Factionalism, Instability, Shorter tenures, Weak PM.

Decode: the UPSC Mains line is "by the mid-18th century India was a spectre of fragmented polity"; the State PCS line is "by 1715 Punjab". Read it as a weak centre, as with coalition governments today.

Why it sticks: the four ills match the four words of the teacher's board; Mains 2017 gets the Misl confederacy as its Punjab example.

8 · Ranjit Singh 01-39 unifies the Misls

Memory hook

Jagir, not Watan: the chiefs held on conditions.

Ranjit Singh 01-39's chiefs got a Jagir with strings; the Watan Jagir of Chapter 5's rebels was a different bargain.

Decode: after the 1801 coronation (Ranjit Singh 01-39), Misl chiefs became tribute-paying vassals with conditional control of land in return for loyalty and tribute: Jagirs, not Watan.

Why it sticks: "Loyalty and tribute, or no land."

8.1 Territorial expansion: rapid and systematic

Memory hook

Ranjit Singh 01-39's date rhyme: 01, 09, 18-19, 34, 38.

Oh-one: the crown. Oh-nine: the line. Eighteen-nineteen: Multan, Kashmir shine. Thirty-four: Peshawar's gate. Thirty-eight: the pact of fate.

Decode: coronation 1801 (Sukerchakia Misl, after Zaman Shah's invasions); Treaty of Amritsar 1809 (Sutlej line); Multan 1818, Kashmir 1819 (back to back); Peshawar 1834; Tripartite Treaty 1838. Chapter ruling: the handout says Lahore 1801 and the slides say Lahore 1799 (from the Bhangi Misl), so anchor on these six dates.

Why it sticks: pairs and a rhyme: 01-09, 18-19, 34-38.

9 · The Treaty of Amritsar (1809)

Memory hook

Treaty of Amritsar 1809: R-M-S-F, "Ranjit Singh 01-39 Met Metcalfe, Shook hands, Freed".

Recognised, Military, Sutlej, Free.

Decode: 1809, Ranjit Singh 01-39 and the EIC (Charles Metcalfe, Resident at Delhi): Recognised as sovereign of Punjab; Military alliance (link to Ring Fence); Sutlej fixed as border; Free expansion on his side. Map: north of the Sutlej = RJ 01-39, south = EIC.

Why it sticks: Metcalfe's name echoes "Met", and R-M-S-F is the order of the four outcomes.

10 · How far did Ranjit Singh 01-39 expand after 1809?

Memory hook

West and north: Multan, Kashmir, then the long wait for Peshawar.

18, 19 back to back; then 34 and a legend: Haria Ragle.

Decode: after 1809, east secured, Ranjit Singh 01-39 turns west and north: 1818 Multan (Afghans removed); 1819 Kashmir (the Marathas retook Punjab in 1758 but not Kashmir; he completed it); 1834 Peshawar, conquered by Hari Singh Nalwa (present-day Khyber Pakhtunkhwa, Durand Line), whose name Afghan mothers used to quiet children.

Why it sticks: Multan and Kashmir are consecutive years; Peshawar needs the one name, Nalwa.

10.1 The 1838 Tripartite Treaty

Memory hook

Tripartite 1838: Russia Always Knocks; Sikhs Cushion.

R-A-K-S-C: the chain from Russia down to the Company.

Decode: Russia → Afghanistan → Khyber Pass → Sikh State → Company State. Three parties (Tri): the Sikh State, the EIC and Shah Shuja of Afghanistan; Shah Shuja is the puppet for British indirect rule; Afghanistan and Punjab are the buffer against the feared Russian invasion.

Why it sticks: the sentence is the board's chain, top to bottom.

11 · What kind of State did Ranjit Singh 01-39 build?

Memory hook

Ranjit Singh 01-39's four-legged charpai.

The Maharaja sits on a charpai with four legs; kick one out and he wobbles.

Decode: legs = Misl chiefs, the Khalsa army, Khatri traders, non-Sikh nobles such as the Dogras; power built by coercion, diplomacy and careful balance (similar to the successor States). Land: only 40% under Jagirdari; from the rest revenue went directly to the King.

Why it sticks: a charpai needs all four legs, and balance is the whole point.

Memory hook

Kardar = the four C's.

Collector, Court, Cop, Customs: one man in the Taluqa.

Decode: Kardar, chief administrative officer of a Taluqa (district), is the revenue Collector, local magistrate (Court), police chief (Cop) and Customs officer. He often acted as an Ijaradar, but the ryot was not to be oppressed by very high LR.

Why it sticks: four jobs, four C's.

Memory hook

Secular cabinet: Fakir for Foreign, Dewan for Dhan.

F-F and D-D, with the Sahajdhari "sahaj" (easy) Sikhs welcomed in.

Decode: Fakir Azizuddin (Muslim) = Foreign Minister; Dewan Dina Nath (Hindu) = Finance Minister. Sahajdhari Sikhs (non-Khalsa) do not maintain the five Ks, and were included. Village autonomy: State authority stopped at the village level (links to the Mahalwari Settlement in Punjab).

Why it sticks: initials pair up, F with Foreign and D with Dhan (money).

Memory hooks: chapter in one breath

Memory hook

From Nanak's langar to the Tripartite pact, one walk.

One Direct Road Home (Nanak, 1469-1539), then two flashpoints: 5th Guru in 1606, 9th in 1675.

The 10th Guru, Gobind Singh 76-08, raises the Khalsa in 1699; Wazir Khan walls the boys (1704), plots the Guru's death (1708), and falls at Chappar Chiri (1710) to Banda Bahadur 08-15, who has COS: Coins, Officials, Seal.

Banda Bahadur 08-15 is lost to the K-D-J DJ (Khatris, Divide-and-rule, Jats): defeated 1715, executed 1716.

A dozen eggs in the Dal Khalsa basket (12 Misls, 1770) wait for a charpai-balancer: Ranjit Singh 01-39, crowned 1801.

Then "01, 09, 18-19, 34, 38": Amritsar's Sutlej line, Multan, Kashmir, Peshawar, and the Tripartite chain Russia-Afghanistan-Khyber-Sikhs-Company.

Decode: ODRH, 5-9-10 = 06-75-99, A-J-Z-F, P-P-P, COS, KDJ, 12 Misls, R-M-S-F, R-A-K-S-C.

Why it sticks: one story from faith to martyrs to State to treaties.

Chapter 7: Already Autonomous States: Rajputs, Travancore, Mysore (32)open chapter

Exam lens: priority and PYQs

Memory hook

Prepare list: J-J-T.

Jaipur, Jantar Mantars, Tipu 82-99: the three J-J-T you must carry.

Decode: Jaipur (1727, grid, first planned city, Vidyadhar Bhattacharya); Jantar Mantars (five observatories); Tipu 82-99 facts (embassies and more). Colachel 1741 is tested in Chapter 8.

Why it sticks: Sawai Jai Singh 99-43 gives the first two J's, Tipu 82-99 the T.

1 · Akbar 56-05 and the Rajputs

Memory hook

Akbar 56-05's Rajput bargain: M-V plus S-T-T.

The chief becomes an MVP (Mansabdar with Vatan Jagir) if he gives S-T-T.

Decode: Akbar 56-05 co-opts the powerful chiefs: Mansabdar rank + Vatan Jagir (home land stays theirs). In return they give Sovereignty (accept the Mughal Emperor), Tribute, Troops. Bonus: they form the Hindu bloc, about 15% of Mansabdars; roughly 20 clans; Akbar 56-05 helps them subdue smaller chiefs.

Why it sticks: MV for what they get, S-T-T for what they pay; 20 clans, 15% share.

2 · Rajputs against Aurangzeb 58-07, and the Marathas

Memory hook

1680: three M's, Mewar, Marwar, Mughal.

Mewar grows, Marwar gets a child king, the Mughal meddles; the rest sit out.

Decode: Mewar under Raj Singh expands, so Aurangzeb 58-07 grows uneasy; 1680 a child comes to the throne of Marwar (astride the Delhi/Agra to Ahmedabad route) and Aurangzeb 58-07 interferes (the trigger); Marwar and Mewar revolt; other chiefs refuse to join, fearing Mewar would dominate them, so it collapses.

Why it sticks: the fear of Mewar is the reason in one word.

Memory hook

Rajputs after Aurangzeb 58-07: 07, 51, 03-05.

Seven: own terms. Fifty-one: Maratha raids. Three-to-five: the Company moves in.

Decode: after 1707 Rajputs act on their own terms (e.g. Sawai Jai Singh 99-43 of Amber, Mansabdar on his own conditions); from 1751 Marathas raid for tribute and interfere in successions but never conquer; after the Second Anglo-Maratha War (1803-05) the EIC replaces them with Subordinate (Subsidiary) Alliances.

Why it sticks: three masters in time order, Mughal, Maratha, EIC.

Memory hook

Subordinate Alliance: keeps the chair, loses the keys.

The raja keeps the throne but hands over the keys of foreign policy and defence, and pays the guard.

Decode: like World History's Protectorate: throne kept; foreign policy and defence surrendered to the EIC; EIC troops stationed, paid for by the Indian ruler. Under suzerainty = loss of external sovereign functions plus symbolic sovereignty.

Why it sticks: one chair, two keys, one bill.

3 · Sawai Jai Singh 99-43 of Amber (1699-1743)

Memory hook

Sawai = "sava": one and a quarter, four hats.

Sawai Jai Singh 99-43 wears four hats: Mansabdar's turban, builder's helmet, astronomer's lens, planner's compass.

Decode: "Sawai" = 1.25 times more able than any contemporary ruler (title from Aurangzeb 58-07). Four roles: Mughal Mansabdar, regional state-builder, astronomer, town planner (teacher's arrows: astronomer and town planner point to Prelims).

Why it sticks: sava = 1.25 is the meaning itself; four hats = four roles.

Memory hook

Malwa slipping: 29, 34, 37, 38.

Governor in 29, Hurda in 34 (fails), term ends 37, Bhopal 38: Malwa gone.

Decode: Sawai Jai Singh 99-43 is Subedar (Governor) of Malwa 1729-37 and of Agra; 1734 Hurda conference of Rajput rulers, a united front against the Marathas, collapses from internal rivalries; 1738 Treaty of Bhopal: Malwa annexed by the Marathas.

Why it sticks: four numbers climb, and each step loses ground.

Memory hook

Last Yajnas: A-V, and the dowry that stayed.

Ashvamedha and Vajapeya: the last A-V of Hindu India's rulers.

Decode: Sawai Jai Singh 99-43 was the last Indian ruler to perform the Ashvamedha and Vajapeya Yajnas (a conscious assertion of Hindu Chakravartin status as Mughal authority collapsed); he also tried to curb dowry and excessive marriage expenditure, with limited success.

Why it sticks: A before V, in the chapter's order.

Memory hook

Jaipur: 27 grid, 76 pink.

In '27 Vidyadhar drew the Vastu grid; in '76 Sawai Ram Singh painted it pink for the Prince of Wales.

Decode: Jaipur founded 1727; designed by Vidyadhar Bhattacharya on Vastu Shastra principles with a grid-pattern layout; the first planned city of modern India; painted pink in 1876 by Sawai Ram Singh to welcome the Prince of Wales.

Why it sticks: the designer comes at 27, the painter at 76, both with a 7.

3.1 The Jantar Mantars and astronomy

Memory hook

Three yantras: Samrat = Second, Jai = Map, Ram = Rise.

Samrat tells the Second, Jai Prakash paints the sky, Ram measures the Rise of the Sun.

Decode: Samrat Yantra = sundial for time (largest sundial in the world, accurate to 2 seconds); Jai Prakash Yantra = celestial mapper; Ram Yantra = altitude meter for the Sun's position. All in stone, not brass, for greater accuracy.

Why it sticks: each name begins with the first letter of its job word, Second, Map, Rise.

Memory hook

Five Jantar Mantars: 24, 34, then U-M-V.

Delhi 24, Jaipur 34 (biggest), then Ujjain, Mathura, Varanasi in a row.

Decode: Delhi (1724), Jaipur (1734, largest, UNESCO World Heritage Site 2010), Ujjain, Mathura, Varanasi. Five in all.

Why it sticks: the two dated ones are 24 and 34, ten apart; the last three go together as U-M-V.

Memory hook

Zij-i-Muhammad Shahi 1733: fresh tables for two old ones.

'33 for the Shah, with '30 sending a delegation to Portugal.

Decode: tables tell where a celestial body will be at a given date and time; the Zij-i-Ulugh Begi (Islamic) and Siddhantic (Hindu) tables had accumulated errors, so fresh observations gave the Zij-i-Muhammad Shahi (1733), dedicated to Md Shah 19-48; he also sent a scientific delegation to Portugal (1730) and commissioned Sanskrit astronomical works.

Why it sticks: two old tables, one new Shah; 30 then 33.

4 · Travancore: Marthanda Varma 29-58

Memory hook

Travancore's trade income goes to M-I-T.

Marthanda Varma 29-58 sends the spice money to MIT: Military, Irrigation, Transport.

Decode: never part of the Mughal Empire (Mughals never expanded far enough south); by the 1740s a centralised State by subjugating local chiefs, with a European-style military; State monopoly over Malabar coast trade; revenues reinvested in Military, Irrigation works, Transport infrastructure.

Why it sticks: MIT = a small State becomes a smart State.

Memory hook

The 58 handshake.

Marthanda Varma 29-58 passes the baton to Rama Varma 58-98 at 58.

Decode: Marthanda Varma 29-58 (military, trade monopoly) then Rama Varma 58-98 (art and scholarship, military declines; Malabar and Calicut lost in 1766; British influence after 1798).

Why it sticks: the codes touch at 58, then end at 98, the year British influence starts.

4.1 Colachel 1741 and the fall of the Dutch

Memory hook

Dutch down the stairs: 41 Kerala, 59 Bengal, 95 South Asia.

Colachel locks them out of Kerala, Bedara of Bengal, 1795 of everywhere.

Decode: since 1663 Dutch Malabar HQ at Cochin (taken from the Portuguese); 1741 Battle of Colachel, Marthanda Varma 29-58 crushes the Dutch East India Company (they become mere merchants in Cochin); 1759 Battle of Bedara (Chinsura), the EIC defeats the Dutch in Bengal; 1795 final expulsion from South Asia (Ceylon).

Why it sticks: the places widen, Kerala, Bengal, South Asia, as the dates climb 41, 59, 95.

5 · Travancore against Mysore and the EIC

Memory hook

Malabar 1766: spice kitchen, yes; Travancore, no.

Pepper, cardamom, ginger: the kitchen Haider Ali 61-82 grabbed in 1766.

Decode: Haider Ali 61-82 annexed Malabar and Calicut in 1766, the heart of the spice trade (pepper, cardamom, ginger); whoever held its ports held a big share of Indian Ocean commerce, so both Mysore and the British wanted it. The first Anglo-Mysore War (1767-69) followed.

Why it sticks: spice kitchen = Malabar; the flag below says Travancore itself was not taken.

Memory hook

Sign in 05, rise in 09.

1805: no pay, the Nairs mutiny, help is sought, the Subsidiary Alliance is signed. 1809: Velu Thampi rises.

Decode: 1805 Nair troops mutiny over unpaid salaries; Travancore seeks British help and signs the Subsidiary Alliance (independence effectively ends); anger at the burden and interference; 1809 Velu Thampi Dalawa (PM) revolts against the British Resident and issues the Kundara Proclamation; suppressed, he takes his own life.

Why it sticks: 05 and 09 are four years apart, and the Alliance comes before the revolt.

Memory hook

Dalawa keeps the sword, Dewan loses it.

Dalawa = PM plus army chief; Dewan under the British = all administration, no military.

Decode: Dalawa = Prime Minister and Chief of the Armed Forces; Dewan (PM of a State subordinated by the British) = head of all administration (revenue, civil, police, criminal justice) while the military sits with the British.

Why it sticks: sword (military) goes with the old title, files and courts go with the new.

6 · Mysore: from Vijayanagar to Haider Ali 61-82

Memory hook

Vijayanagar: 1336 born, 1565 hit, 1646 gone.

Three-three-six birth; fifteen-sixty-five, Talikota blow; sixteen-forty-six, last breath.

Decode: Vijayanagar Empire 1336-1646 (Mysore was part of it); 1565 Battle of Talikota (Rakkasa-Tangadi), a coalition of Deccan Sultanates defeats it, the operational end; the dynasty lingers to 1646. Then Wodeyars gain autonomy; Chikka Devaraja 72-04 Wodeyar makes Mysore powerful.

Why it sticks: start, blow, end: one number each.

Memory hook

Haider Ali 61-82 takes power in 61.

The year he seized power is the year his code begins: 61.

Decode: by 1761 the Wodeyars were figureheads under corrupt PM NanjRaj; in 1761 Haider Ali 61-82, a junior officer (the Wodeyars had helped pay off his family's debts), overthrew NanjRaj and kept the Wodeyar as nominal head. Pattern: the king becomes a puppet as personality weakens (like Peshwa and Chhatrapati).

Why it sticks: the code 61-82 starts exactly in 1761, so the code itself holds the year.

6.1 Haider Ali 61-82's military machine

Memory hook

Risala = the three C's.

Commander (the King's), Chain of command, Complete (self-sufficient).

Decode: French-assisted army on a European model; each Risala (regiment) has a commander directly appointed by the King (personal loyalty), a clear chain of command, and is self-sufficient with its own weapons, transport and supplies (like Scindia's Campoo); result is fast, efficient regiments capable of independent action.

Why it sticks: three C's, three features, one regiment.

Memory hook

Poligars have Power, Deshmukhs collect Dues.

P for Power, D for Dues: Haider Ali 61-82 spent about twenty years breaking both.

Decode: Poligars = local chiefs and landlords with land, temple wealth and their own armed men, the real rulers of the countryside since Vijayanagar; Deshmukhs = revenue collectors who functioned like primary zamindars. Subjugating them ended Non-State power.

Why it sticks: one letter each gives the one-line difference.

6.2 How Mysore funded this machine: Military Fiscalism

Memory hook

Khalisa = Khazana; Jagir = Jebs of nobles.

Khalisa feeds the Khazana (treasury); Jagirdari fills the Jebs (pockets) of nobles.

Decode: Khalisa land = revenue goes directly to the State treasury (Mysore expanded it); Jagirdari land = revenue assigned to nobles or commanders (Mysore minimised it). Deshmukhs removed; State officials collect directly on salaries, like replacing a commission agent with a salaried employee.

Why it sticks: K-K (Khalisa, Khazana) and J-J (Jagir, Jebs).

Memory hook

Surveys by SCIP; farm help by C-C-S.

SCIP: Soil fertility, Crop type, Irrigation access, Past production. CCS: Canals, Cash crops, Silk.

Decode: detailed field surveys assessed land revenue by soil fertility, crop type, irrigation access and past production trends (prevented over-assessment); investment in agriculture = canals repaired and built, cash crops promoted, sericulture introduced. All of this is Military Fiscalism: State wealth as a foundation for military power.

Why it sticks: two short codes, one for assessing and one for investing; richer peasants mean a larger tax base.

7 · Tipu 82-99 Sultan (1782-99): innovations and why he still lost

Memory hook

Tipu 82-99's trade list: S-S-R-P-S-C.

"Sandalwood, Sulphur, Rice, Pepper, Silk, Cardamom": Sandy Sam Runs, Packs Silk, Cardamom.

Decode: his government-owned trading company, modelled on the EIC, had 30 trading centres inside and outside India (including Muscat, Oman) and traded in sandalwood, sulphur, rice, pepper, silk and cardamom.

Why it sticks: the sentence follows the list order, six items, six words.

Memory hook

Tipu 82-99's rockets: iron case, 2 km, Congreve copied.

Iron does not burst; cardboard does. The British noticed.

Decode: iron-cased rocket artillery developed by Haider Ali 61-82 and Tipu 82-99, 100% indigenous, range 2 km; iron cylinders did not burst under pressure (unlike cardboard-cased fireworks known for centuries in India and China); William Congreve's rockets (early 19th century) drew on Tipu 82-99's designs.

Why it sticks: "iron = no burst, 2 km"; a cardboard case is just a firework.

Memory hook

1785: independence, then ask the Khalifa.

Eighty-five: Tipu 82-99 cuts both strings (Wodeyars and Mughal) and asks the Khalifa for a sanad.

Decode: 1785 Tipu 82-99 formally declared independence from the Wodeyars and the Mughal Emperor and requested a sanad from the Khalifa recognising him as independent of Mughal authority.

Why it sticks: two strings cut, one new authority asked.

7.1 Tipu 82-99 and Revolutionary France

Memory hook

Tipu 82-99 Joined, Napoleon Never helped.

T-J-N: Tree of Liberty, Jacobin Club, Napoleon's no.

Decode: Tipu 82-99 sent an ambassador to France in the 1790s (the French Revolution began 1789; the 23-year wars ran 1792-1815; in 1798 Napoleon attacked Egypt); he exchanged communications with Napoleon, planted a Tree of Liberty and joined the Jacobin Club; No military support came; the British were alarmed, a reason for the Fourth Anglo-Mysore War of 1799.

Why it sticks: two things he did, one thing he never got.

Memory hook

Prelims 2001: three fighters, one diplomat.

Haider Ali 61-82 hit the field, Mir Qasim 60-63 fought at Buxar, Shah Alam II 60-06 fell; Tipu 82-99 sent envoys: F-T-E.

Decode: answer (d) Tipu 82-99 Sultan, embassies in France, Turkey, Egypt (plus new coinage and the Mauludi Lunisolar Calendar). Wrong options: (a) Haider Ali 61-82 built the French-model army with Risalas; (b) Mir Qasim 60-63 fought the EIC at Buxar (1764); (c) Shah Alam II 60-06, Mughal Emperor reduced after Buxar.

Why it sticks: the other three are all battlefield names; "embassy" belongs to the one who looked abroad.

7.2 Why Mysore lost

Memory hook

Four Anglo-Mysore Wars: 67, 80, 90, 99 and lengths 2-4-2-1.

Sixty-seven, eighty, ninety, ninety-nine: two years, four years, two years, one year.

Decode: 1767-69, 1780-84, 1790-92, 1799. The First followed Haider Ali 61-82's 1766 annexation of Malabar and Calicut (the EIC feared losing coastal trade access); the Fourth ended with Tipu 82-99's death at Seringapatam, Mysore's power broken. Why Mysore lost: ambition to dominate all South India (Vijayanagar 2.0) while constant war drained the State faster than Military Fiscalism refilled it.

Why it sticks: the start years climb by 13, 10, 9, and the last war is the shortest, a single year.

Memory hook

Trap-proof: M-C yes, Travancore no.

Malabar-Calicut taken, Travancore too tough: follow the slide, not the handout's section 7.

Decode: the handout's section 7 says Haider Ali 61-82 "invaded Travancore and annexed Malabar and Calicut"; the teacher's bracket says he "could not successfully invade Travancore". Chapter ruling: use the slide's wording.

Why it sticks: two places lost (M and C), one place held (T).

Memory hooks: chapter in one breath

Memory hook

Three states that were never really Mughal, one walk.

Akbar 56-05 gave the Rajputs an MVP deal (S-T-T); the 1680 M's (Mewar, Marwar, Mughal) broke it; then 07, 51, 03-05 brought Marathas and the Company, with Sawai Jai Singh 99-43 wearing four hats and building Jaipur ('27 grid, '76 pink).

South, Marthanda Varma 29-58 spends the spice money on MIT, crushes the Dutch at Colachel (41), hands on at 58; Rama Varma 58-98 loses Malabar (1766); sign in 05, rise in 09 with Velu Thampi.

Mysore: Vijayanagar 1336, 1565, 1646; Haider Ali 61-82 takes power in 61, builds Risalas and Khalisa wealth.

Tipu 82-99 adds trade (S-S-R-P-S-C), rockets, embassies F-T-E, Tree and Jacobin; four wars 67, 80, 90, 99 end at Seringapatam.

Decode: S-T-T; MMM; 07-51-03/05; 27 and 76; MIT; 41; 05-09; 61; SCIP, CCS; T-J-N; 2-4-2-1.

Why it sticks: one tour: north to south to Mysore, with a number for each stop.

Chapter 8: The Advent of Europeans (57)open chapter

1 · Asia, the economic power of the world

Memory hook

Three prizes, three places.

"Islands give Spice, India gives Cloth, China gives Silk (and tea)."

Decode: Islands = Indonesia (Java, Sumatra) for spices; India = textiles (Malabar also spices); China = silk and tea. Middlemen in order: Arabs on the Arabian Sea leg, then Genoa and Venice across the Mediterranean.

Why it sticks: I-I-C is the same order as the sentence; every handoff meant extra profit for a middleman.

Europe was not wealthy; spices and the 800% profit

Memory hook

Eight-hundred and gold.

Picture Da Gama (1498) unloading a ship where every sack sells for 8 times its cost, and a kilo of clove sits on the scale against a kilo of gold.

Decode: 1498 cargo = 800% profit; by the 1600s 1 kg clove = 1 kg gold; spices also kept winter meat edible (clove and pepper did the job).

Why it sticks: "clove = gold" matches the handout's own joke about "spitting gold" as children.

Forex versus domestic currency, and the triangle

Memory hook

Cloth goes, spice comes.

"Buy cloth, Sell cloth, Spice back, Sell spice" = B-S-S-S.

Decode: (1) EIC buys cloth from India; (2) sells that cloth to Indonesia; (3) Indonesian goods (spices) come back to the EIC; (4) EIC sells them to the rest of Europe for profit.

Why it sticks: Indonesia wanted nothing from Europe but accepted Indian cloth, so "to buy spice you first need cloth".

2 · The Ottoman blockade (1453) and the Age of Discovery

Memory hook

A Lazy Guide Crosses.

"Ahoy! Look, Geography Carries!" = A-L-G-C.

Decode: A = Astrolabe (location) and magnetic compass (direction); L = Latitudes and longitudes (modern world map); G = Geography as a formal discipline; C = Caravel (sails at an angle against the wind).

Why it sticks: four gifts, four letters; and 1453 is the "why": the Ottomans took Constantinople, so the land routes were effectively blockaded.

3 · Treaty of Tordesillas, 1494

Memory hook

Spain Sleeps West, Portugal Pushes East.

Columbus 1492 sails west; two years later (1494) Pope Alexander VI draws the line.

Decode: S-W = Spain gets lands WEST of the line; P-E = Portugal gets lands EAST; line = about 1,185 miles west of the Cape Verde Islands (1-1-8-5, "one-one-eight-five"); Brazil's eastern tip pokes over the line, so Brazil is the only Portuguese colony in the Americas.

Why it sticks: the Portuguese went east round the Cape of Good Hope to the East Indies, so "Portugal = East" feels natural.

4.0 · Da Gama at Calicut (1498)

Memory hook

Gama = Calicut, Cabral = Cochin.

"Gama Greets Calicut (98), Cabral Burns it, then Builds in Cochin (00)."

Decode: 1498 Da Gama at Calicut, trade permission from the Zamorin, NO factory (teacher: "NOT COCHIN"); 1500 Cabral's first factory at Calicut is destroyed, so he sails south; 1500 first PERMANENT factory at Cochin under the Raja of Cochin.

Why it sticks: years go 98 then 00, and "Ga-Ca" (Gama-Calicut) versus "Ca-Co" (Cabral-Cochin) keeps the pairs apart.

Why trade, not empire? (the teacher's board)

Memory hook

West coast, north to south.

"Dear Sir, Do Bring Goa Mangoes; Calicut Cochin Tea."

Decode: Dear = Diu; Sir = Surat; Do = Daman; Bring = Bombay; Goa = Goa; Mangoes = Mahe; Calicut = Calicut; Cochin = Cochin; Tea = Travancore.

Why it sticks: nine words, nine places, in the teacher's exact order.

4.1 · Structure of trade: a State enterprise

Memory hook

Who owns the company?

"Portugal = PSU; English and Dutch = Private; French = Private, then PSU."

Decode: Estado da India = State enterprise, the Crown was the trader. English EIC and Dutch VOC = private joint-stock companies. French EIC began private and was later nationalised (a PSU).

Why it sticks: only the French company changes sides, from private to public.

Depot, Factory, Settlement

Memory hook

Store, Office, State.

"Depot stores, Factory trades, Settlement rules."

Decode: Depot = godown for goods waiting for the ship (think bus depot; no L-E-J, no tax). Factory = office, factors, farmans, often a fort (e.g. Fort Manuel). Settlement = territory with L-E-J, taxes and troops. Settlement examples climb 10, 74, 90: Goa (post-1510), Pondicherry (post-1674), Calcutta (post-1690).

Why it sticks: only the Settlement can collect and consume land revenue, the way a State does.

Farman, Parwana, Nishan

Memory hook

F-P-N goes down the family tree.

"Farman = Father-king, Parwana = Province governor, Nishan = Next king (prince)."

Decode: Farman = Imperial or Royal order from the King or Emperor; Parwana = Local order from a Provincial Governor; Nishan = order from a Prince of the royal blood.

Why it sticks: Top to bottom of power: King, Governor, Prince.

4.2 · Early factories, the first fort and the aggression (1498-1503)

Memory hook

Four steps in five years (98, 00, 02, 03).

Story: "Gama comes, Cabral burns and builds, Gama avenges, Albuquerque 09-15 walls it."

Decode: 1498 Da Gama at Calicut (no factory); 1500 Cabral, first permanent factory at Cochin; 1502 Da Gama's 2nd voyage, bombards Calicut and massacres fishermen to avenge Cabral's men; 1503 Albuquerque 09-15 (then a naval commander) builds Fort Manuel at Cochin, the first European fort in India.

Why it sticks: Cochin is both the first factory (1500) and first fort (1503), and stays capital until Goa in 1530.

4.3 · Blue Water Policy and the Cartaz System (Almeida 05-09, 1505-09)

Memory hook

Diu 1509: three fight, two back.

"G-M-Z fight, O-V back" = "Go Make Zamorin pay; Old Venice backs."

Decode: Fighters = Gujarat, Mamluk Egypt, Zamorin of Calicut; backers = Ottoman (troops and galleys) and Venice. Winner = Almeida 05-09, whose last year (09) is also the year of Diu (1509): it was his farewell victory.

Why it sticks: the battle was for the Arabian Sea, not for the town of Diu; Cartaz = toll booth on the sea (pay for a pass or be attacked).

4.4 · Afonso de Albuquerque 09-15 (2nd Governor, 1509-15)

Memory hook

Almeida 05-09 = Ocean, Albuquerque 09-15 = Annexed.

"Almeida 05-09 rules the Ocean; Albuquerque 09-15 Acquires Goa."

Decode: Almeida 05-09 = 1st Governor, Blue Water Policy, Cartaz, Diu 1509. Albuquerque 09-15 = 2nd Governor, annexed Goa from Bijapur in 1510 (the exception to Blue Water), Luso-Indian intermarriage. Hand-over year: Almeida 05-09's 09 is Albuquerque 09-15's 09; and 1510 sits right inside his 09-15 span.

Why it sticks: A for Albuquerque 09-15, A for Annex.

4.5 · Peak under Nuno da Cunha 29-38 (Governor 1529-38)

Memory hook

Nuno's 30-34-35.

"Thirty: move house. Thirty-four: Bassein. Thirty-five: Diu. Bahadur gave Both."

Decode: Nuno da Cunha 29-38 shifted the capital from Cochin to Goa in 1530; acquired Bassein (1534) then Diu (1535), both from Bahadur Shah of Gujarat (B before D, Bassein before Diu). Teacher's extra: Bombay 1534 went with the Bassein deal.

Why it sticks: Prelims 2023 asked who surrendered Diu, and the answer is the "B" in the rhyme, Bahadur Shah.

4.6 · Religious intolerance: Inquisition and Francis Xavier

Memory hook

Died in China, resting in Goa.

"Shangchuan is where he stopped; Bom Jesus is where he's propped."

Decode: Xavier in India from 1542, died 1552 on Shangchuan Island (off China); body later brought to Goa (Basilica of Bom Jesus). 42 to 52 = ten years in India. The Goa Inquisition (1560-1812) starts 8 years after his death: he "led" to it (teacher's arrow).

Why it sticks: Prelims 2021 tested death versus burial, so say "death = Shangchuan" before you read the options.

4.7 · Portuguese contribution: New World crops

Memory hook

Five Portuguese gifts; two late guests.

"Take Red Pepper, Papaya, Pineapple, Guava" = T-R-P-P-G.

Decode: Tobacco, Red chillies, Papaya, Pineapple, Guava came via the Portuguese. Cocoa and Rubber (C-R, "Came after, with the Raj") are New World plants too, but arrived only in the British period.

Why it sticks: Prelims 2025 asked about papaya, pineapple, guava: all three, so the answer was "all of them".

4.8 · Why did the Portuguese decline?

Memory hook

Five causes: P-R-E-E-I.

"Poor Rulers Eat English Idlis."

Decode: P = Political (Marathas rise; 1739 they defeated the Portuguese); R = Religious backlash (Jesuits, Inquisition 1560-1812); E = Economic and administrative decay (corruption, personal trade); E = External competition (English and Dutch); I = Imperial overextension (Brazil, no support from Lisbon).

Why it sticks: five words for five causes, in the handout's order i to v.

Table T1: Portuguese factories, forts and key permissions

Memory hook

Teacher's later losses and the coast split.

"Go Konkan, Come Malabar; Bombay sixty-one, Manuel sixty-three."

Decode: Goa leads the Konkan coast, Cochin the Malabar coast. 1661 Bombay goes to the British; 1663 Fort Manuel (Cochin) is lost to the Dutch; Marathas beat the Portuguese in 1736 and take Salsette and Bassein in 1739 (3-6, then 3-9). Last act: 1961, Goa, Daman, Diu annexed by India.

Why it sticks: 61 then 63 is the British-then-Dutch order; 36 then 39 are the Maratha pair.

5 · How the Dutch and English broke the Portuguese-Spanish monopoly

Memory hook

Union first, Armada next.

"Eighty joins, Eighty-eight sinks": 1580 Portugal joins Spain; 1588 the Armada sinks.

Decode: By 1580 Portugal was in a political union with Spain, so when the British Navy defeated the Spanish Armada in 1588 in the Atlantic, Portuguese routes opened too. British and Dutch ships could now reach Asia via the Cape of Good Hope; first target Indonesia, India came later.

Why it sticks: 80 and 88 are 8 years apart, and the same "8" repeats.

Memory hook

State = T, Company = P.

"State has T and L-E-J; Company has P."

Decode: State = Territory motive (defend, annex), military, may wage war, coins, currency and taxes, and L-E-J (Legislative, Executive, Judicial). Company = Profit motive for shareholders, needs property. Charter = the founding document, issued by the State (Monarchy or Parliament).

Why it sticks: later the EIC (a Company) got State-like powers, which is exactly why it could build an empire.

6 · The Dutch VOC

Memory hook

Mad Pandas Sip Black Kadak Chai, Coffee.

Order of Dutch posts, M-P-S-B-K-C-C.

Decode: Masulipatnam 1605 (first factory); Pulicat 1610 (first fort, Geldria); Surat 1616; Bimilipatam 1641; Karikal 1645; Chinsura 1653; Cochin 1663 (captured from the Portuguese). Years run 05, 10, 16, then 41, 45, 53, 63. Also: 1602 has a "6": six smaller companies merged into the VOC.

Why it sticks: the teacher ticked 1602, 1605, 1653, 1663 and 1741, so those are the ones to say aloud.

7 · The English EIC and the Royal Charter of 1600

Memory hook

Mo-Go-Man: three gifts of 1600.

"Monopoly, Gold, Management."

Decode: Mo = Monopoly over England's trade with the East Indies for 15 years (NOT "trade with India"; the teacher corrected this); Go = right to carry Gold (forex) out of England; Man = Management by a Governor, a Deputy Governor and 24 members (later the Court of Directors).

Why it sticks: Elizabeth I 58-03 signed on 31 Dec 1600; 215 merchants; the 4th joint-stock company in England.

Memory hook

Per-trip ticket versus permanent pot.

"EIC 1600 = pay, sail, share, repeat; VOC 1602 = one pot, dividends."

Decode: EIC in 1600 worked per VOYAGE (collect money, sell goods, share all profits, start again). The VOC (1602) had permanent capital and gave dividends (retain, re-invest, assets, dividends). The EIC became a continuous joint-stock fund only in 1657 (Cromwell 51-60).

Why it sticks: think of a one-trip cab share versus a company that keeps its shareholders' money.

8 · Charters until 1793: the EIC's changing legal character

Memory hook

Lancaster loots first.

"Lancaster sailed to Spice Islands, not India, and stole his first profit."

Decode: First voyage 1601-03 under James Lancaster went to Indonesia (Java and Sumatra), set up the first EIC factory there, and looted the Portuguese ship Sao Tome in the Strait of Malacca; the fleet returned in 1603.

Why it sticks: "first profit = piracy" (Dalrymple's point in the chapter).

Memory hook

Charter ladder: E-J-C-C-P.

"Every Jolly Child Climbs Parliament."

Decode: Elizabeth I 58-03 (1600, 15-year monopoly); James I 03-25 (1609, perpetual); Cromwell 51-60 (1657, permanent joint-stock fund); Charles II 60-85 (1661, quasi-State powers); Parliament (1793 onwards, Acts renew the Charter).

Why it sticks: the teacher's "imp" years are 1600, 1661 and 1793 (the first, fourth and last rung); 1609, 1657 and 1676 are "not imp".

Memory hook

Kings out, kings in.

"Charles I 25-49 loses his head the year his reign ends (49); Cromwell 51-60's 51-60 ends the year Charles II 60-85's 60-85 begins (60)."

Decode: English Civil War 1642-51; Charles I 25-49 beheaded 1649; Republic under Cromwell 51-60; 1660 Restoration; Charles II 60-85. Cromwell 51-60's 1657 Charter ended the per-voyage system.

Why it sticks: each reign's last two digits chain into the next: 49, then 51-60, then 60-85.

Memory hook

1661 = FTWC, "Fort, Troops, War, Court".

Charles II 60-85 turns merchants into a "semi-State".

Decode: F = build Forts; T = command Troops; W = make War and peace; C = civil and criminal jurisdiction (Court). The right to mint money is later (1676, "not imp").

Why it sticks: four powers of a Ruling Power, four letters; this is how a Company could build an empire.

8.7 · Why couldn't Indian merchants dominate the new trade routes?

Memory hook

N-C-S: "Needs Cash and Ships".

Why Indian merchants could not dominate the sea routes.

Decode: N = Naval power (no armed ocean-going fleet; teacher: MILITARY NAVY); C = Capital (VOC 1602 and EIC by 1657 pooled big joint-stock funds); S = State backing (Mughals after Akbar 56-05 built a territorial army, not a navy).

Why it sticks: three missing things, three letters.

9 · The Danish East India Company

Memory hook

Danes: 20, 24, 25, then 55.

"Tranquebar twenty, Pondicherry twenty-four, Pipli twenty-five; Serampore fifty-five."

Decode: 1616 Charter of Christian IV 88-48 (12-year monopoly); 1620 Tranquebar (Tamil Nadu, first factory, Fort Dansborg, HQ till 1845); 1624 Pondicherry (Roland Crappe, brief); 1625 Masulipatnam, Pipli, Balasore; 1755 Serampore (parwana from Alivardi Khan 40-56); 1799-1800 Serampore Mission Press (William Carey). Tranquebar = Carnatic, Serampore = Bengal (teacher's labels).

Why it sticks: arrival order is "P-E-V-D": Portugal 1500, EIC 1600, VOC 1602, Danes 1616 (order of company founding); the teacher ticked 1616, 1620, 1755 and 1799-1800.

10 · How the EIC broke Portuguese dominance: Hawkins, Swally and Surat

Memory hook

Hawkins Hurt, Best Beat, Roe Reaped.

"H-B-R: 1608 fail, 1612 fight, 1615-19 rights."

Decode: Hawkins (1608, Surat factory bid at Jahangir 05-27's court) got Mansab 400 and a Jagir but was expelled under Portuguese pressure; Thomas Best won the Battle of Swally (1612) against the Portuguese; Roe (1615-19) got the Royal Farman of 1618 with liberal trading rights.

Why it sticks: Portugal had to be beaten militarily first, so the fighter (Best) comes between the two diplomats.

Memory hook

12 is the Sword, 13 is the Seal.

"Swally 12 = sword; Surat 13 = signed paper."

Decode: 1612 Battle of Swally (Best, military victory, marked "imp") and a temporary local permission; 1613 Jahangir 05-27's Royal Farman making the Surat factory permanent. Prelims 2006 asked the 1613 factory: Surat.

Why it sticks: S-S: Swally, Sword; Surat, Seal.

11 · The Royal Farman of 1618 and Thomas Roe (1615-19)

Memory hook

Roe's three: "Aa Aa Bharuch".

A-A-B: Agra, Ahmedabad, Broach (Bharuch).

Decode: Roe (ambassador, arrived 1615, stayed to 1619) got the Royal Farman of 1618 and new factories in 1616-19 at Agra, Ahmedabad and Broach; with Surat these formed the Surat Presidency (1618), the first Presidency. Hugli 1631-32: Shah Jahan 27-58 had Qasim Khan eject the Portuguese for piracy and the slave trade.

Why it sticks: Prelims 2015 and 2021 asked first-quarter 17th-century factories: only Broach was right ("bas Bharuch", Hindi bas = only); Chicacole and Trichinopoly had none.

12 · Anglo-Portuguese Treaty of 1661: dowry diplomacy

Memory hook

The Bride's Basket: B-T-B.

"Bombay, Tangier, Bucks (500,000 pounds)."

Decode: Treaty of 1661, signed at Whitehall: Charles II 60-85 marries Catherine of Braganza. Dowry = Bombay + Tangier (Morocco) + 500,000 pounds cash; England protects Portugal's remaining possessions (Goa, Daman, Diu, till 1961). Seven years later, 1668, Bombay goes to the EIC at 10 pounds a year; Bombay Presidency in 1687, about 20 years after 1668 (teacher's "20 yrs" bracket).

Why it sticks: 61 wedding, 68 rent, 87 HQ; Bombay is also a cluster of seven islands, and 1687 ends in 7.

13 · The Dutch: rise, dominance and fall in Asia

Memory hook

Dutch chain: 23, 58, 63, 67.

"Amboyna kills, Ceylon falls, Cochin captured, Breda divides."

Decode: 1623 Amboyna Massacre ends English ambitions in Indonesia; 1658 Dutch remove the Portuguese from Ceylon; 1663 Cochin captured; 1667 Treaty of Breda ends the Anglo-Dutch wars (1654-67): the war ends in the treaty year.

Why it sticks: Breda is a swap. British leave Indonesia to the Dutch, the Dutch leave the English alone in India, and each keeps only a minor presence in the other's home zone (British: Singapore, Malaya).

Memory hook

Cochin is a Corner, not the Capital.

"Pulicat Passes the keys to Nagapattinam; Cochin and Chinsura are Corner offices."

Decode: Main HQ = Pulicat 1610-90, then Nagapattinam 1690-1795 (1610 and 1690 both start 16-, one pair apart in the last two digits). Cochin = Malabar regional HQ only. Chinsura = Bengal regional HQ. Teacher's Amazon image: Bengaluru = main HQ, Gurgaon = regional HQ.

Why it sticks: both "C" towns are Corner offices; no "C" town is the main HQ.

Memory hook

Dutch down: 41, 59, 95, 99.

"Colachel South, Bedara Bengal; Kew letters, then the grave."

Decode: 1741 Colachel, Marthanda Varma 29-58 of Travancore beats the Dutch; 1759 Bedara (Chinsura), British EIC beats them in Bengal; 1795 Kew Letters hand posts to the British; 1799 VOC bankrupt and dissolved. 59 and 95 are mirror images, which keeps Bedara and Kew in place.

Why it sticks: Colachel is the south of Kerala (teacher's sketch: Cochin top, Colachel bottom).

15 · Why Ceylon did not become part of British India: the Crown Colony

Memory hook

India Inc. versus Ceylon Crown.

"India = Company's colony; Ceylon = Crown's colony."

Decode: India was ruled by the EIC (a commercial entity with State powers). Ceylon: captured from the Dutch in 1795-96, ceded in the Treaty of Amiens (1802), then the EIC was removed and the British State ruled it directly through an appointed Governor.

Why it sticks: the teacher says the Ceylon story is "not imp", but the meaning of Crown Colony is "imp": ruled directly by the British State.

16 · What were Presidencies?

Memory hook

Presidency order: S-M-B-C.

"Sweet Mangoes Beat Cherries."

Decode: Surat 1618 (first, west), Madras 1652 (London order; 1653 implemented, south), Bombay 1687 (west again, replaces Surat), Calcutta 1699 (1699/1700 in the handout, east). Teacher's same sketch: S, M, B, C.

Why it sticks: directions go west, south, west, east; years end 18, 52, 87, 99.

Memory hook

Madras High Court.

"Bombay's Marathas, Calcutta's Bengal, and Madras gets M-H-C."

Decode: Presidency starts as a trade HQ (a big town); annexations turn it into a province. Bombay grew from the Marathas; Calcutta from the Bengal Suba; Madras from Mysore, Hyderabad and the Carnatic (M-H-C).

Why it sticks: "Madras High Court" is a real phrase that spells M-H-C, and the Governor goes from regional manager to ruler (Subadar, CM).

17.1 · Madras Presidency (from 1652/1653)

Memory hook

Day in 1639, London 52, ship 53.

"Francis Day buys at daybreak, 1639."

Decode: First EIC factory in India = Masulipatnam, 1611, temporary. 1639: Francis Day buys Madraspatanam from the Hindu Nayakas (Vijayanagara governors, 1336-1646); Fort St George built 1639-44 (first EIC fort). Presidency: ordered in London 1652, implemented in India 1653 (the ship takes a year).

Why it sticks: both 1639 and 1611 sit in the "first" bracket; 52 then 53 is just one step.

17.3 · Calcutta Presidency (from 1699/1700)

Memory hook

Su-Go-Ko, "Sudoku" of Calcutta.

Sutanuti, Gobindapur, Kolikata = Su-Go-Ko.

Decode: three villages (zamindari bought 1698 from the Sabarna Roy Chowdhury family); Hugli 1651 first (nishan, Shah Shuja), Sutanuti 1690, zamindari 1698, Presidency of Fort William 1699/1700; Fort William named for William III 89-02. Bengal = 60% of EIC trade: Rs 100 of goods bought, Rs 60 from Bengal.

Why it sticks: the years climb 51, 90, 98, 99; the three village names fit one word.

18 · Chronological reference: EIC factories and permissions (1600-1717)

Memory hook

Eleven, Twelve, Thirteen: M-S-S.

"Gyarah, Barah, Terah": Masulipatnam, Swally, Surat.

Decode: 1611 Masulipatnam (first, temporary factory); 1612 Swally (Thomas Best); 1613 Surat (first permanent factory, farman of Jahangir 05-27). Then Roe 1615-19 and Surat Presidency 1618.

Why it sticks: Hindi counting 11-12-13 is the chain.

Memory hook

East march: B-H-K-D.

"Be Happy, Keep Dancing."

Decode: Balasore and Hariharpur 1633 (first factory in east India); Hugli 1651 (first in Bengal, nishan from Shah Shuja); Kasimbazar 1658 (trading hub, many merchants); Dacca 1668.

Why it sticks: the years climb 33, 51, 58, 68; Hugli is the "first in Bengal", Balasore the "first in the east".

19 · The French in India and the story of Pondicherry

Memory hook

French pairs: 68-69, 73-74, 23-24.

"Soft Mangoes Cost Plenty, Yellow Mangoes Keep."

Decode: Surat 1668 (farman of Aurangzeb 58-07, 1667), Masulipatnam 1669 (Golconda), Chandarnagar 1673 (Shaista Khan 64-88), Pondicherry 1674 (Bijapur; the teacher's tick), Yanam 1723 (Nizam-ul-Mulk 24-48), Mahe 1724, Karaikal 1739 (Pratap Singh 39-63). Last: 1949 Chandernagore, 1954 the rest.

Why it sticks: the years come in pairs, 68-69, 73-74, 23-24; Karaikal's giver Pratap Singh 39-63 starts in 39, the same year as the grant.

Memory hook

Codes that match the year.

"Martin 01 makes the capital; La Bourdonnais 35-46 46 helps take Madras."

Decode: Francois Martin 01-06 makes Pondicherry capital in 1701 and builds Fort Louis; La Bourdonnais 35-46 (Mauritius, proper control 1735) helps Dupleix 42-54 capture Madras in 1746, 1st Carnatic War (1746-48).

Why it sticks: Martin's code starts at his year (01 = 1701); La Bourdonnais 35-46's code ends at his big year (46 = 1746).

Memory hook

Mahe = Malayali = West.

"P-K-Y look East; M looks West."

Decode: Puducherry and Karaikal (East coast, inside Tamil Nadu), Yanam (East coast, inside Andhra Pradesh), Mahe (West coast, Arabian Sea, inside Kerala). UT formed 1962; Chandernagore 1949, Pondicherry 1954.

Why it sticks: Mahe's Malayali (Kerala) link makes it the odd one out on the West coast.

Memory hook

Pass Dosa For Dad, Bro!

P-D-F-D-B: who held Pondicherry, in order.

Decode: Portuguese (16th century), Danes (1624), French (1674), Dutch (1693), British (1761, again 1793, back to the French in 1814); India 1954. The SECOND was the Danes, not the French.

Why it sticks: Prelims 2010: answer (a) Only 1 (the chapter's ruling, over the board's tick on (d)); "occupied" counts even a very temporary occupation.

20 · Who came when: a master comparison

Memory hook

Charter says E-D, Factory says D-E.

"Founding: English before Dutch. First factory: Dutch before English."

Decode: Founding order = Portugal 1500, EIC 1600, VOC 1602, Danes 1616, French. First-factory order (use unless the question says "founded") = Portuguese 1498-1500, Dutch 1605, English 1611, Danish 1620, French 1664/1668.

Why it sticks: the English and Dutch swap places between the two lists; the French stay last in both (Prelims 2007: answer French).

PYQ 1 · Prelims 1995: Hughly piracy (slides 22 and 23)

Memory hook

Portuguese Pirates at Hugli.

"P for Pirates, P for Portuguese."

Decode: Prelims 1995 answer (a): the Portuguese misused trade privileges for piracy and the slave trade at Hugli; Shah Jahan 27-58 had his Bengal governor Qasim Khan eject them in 1631-32. British came to Hugli later, in 1651.

Why it sticks: French (Chandernagore) and Danes (Tranquebar, Serampore) were just trading.

PYQ 2 · Prelims 2003: European entry, the NOT-correct statement (slides 24 and 25)

Memory hook

Goa 15-10 inside 09-15.

"Albuquerque 09-15 took Goa in fifteen-ten, not fourteen-ninety-nine."

Decode: Prelims 2003 NOT-correct statement is (a): Goa was taken in 1510 (from Bijapur), not 1499; Da Gama reached Calicut in 1498 and only traded.

Why it sticks: 1510 sits right inside the 09-15 span, one year after he starts.

PYQ 5 · Prelims 2007: the first British fort (slides 30 and 31)

Memory hook

George Grew it, David Dealt for it.

"Built = George. Bought = David."

Decode: Prelims 2007, underline "constructed": Fort St George (Madras, built 1639-44) is the first fort built by the EIC. Fort St David (Cuddalore) was purchased in 1690; Fort William (Calcutta) was built later, 1696-1700; Fort St Angelo (Kannur) is Portuguese, 1505.

Why it sticks: St David was a deal, so it fails the word "constructed".

PYQ 8 · Prelims 2012: what the English introduced (slides 36 and 37)

Memory hook

"Sab Pehle": all were earlier.

S-B-P = Sher Shah 40-45, Babur 26-30, Portugal.

Decode: Prelims 2012 asked what the English introduced "for the first time": revenue assessment goes to Sher Shah 40-45 (and Todar Mal); mobile cannons to Babur 26-30 (Panipat 1526); tobacco and red chillies to the Portuguese. Answer (d) None.

Why it sticks: Hindi "sab pehle" = everything came earlier, so the English introduced nothing first.

PYQ 9 and PYQ 13 · Prelims 2015 and its repeat in Prelims 2021 (slides 38 to 41)

Memory hook

Quarter-century clock: "bas Bharuch".

Window = 1600-1625; only Broach (Bharuch) fits.

Decode: Roe-era factories 1616-19 were Agra, Ahmedabad, Broach. Surat 1613 and Masulipatnam 1611 are inside the window; Madras 1639 misses it. Chicacole and Trichinopoly had no EIC factory. Prelims 2015 and 2021: answer (a) Only 1.

Why it sticks: bas = only; one answer, one town.

PYQ 10 · Prelims 2018: Bengal exports (slides 46 and 47)

Memory hook

Bengal sends C-S-S-O, never "raw".

"Cotton cloth, Silk, Saltpetre, Opium."

Decode: Prelims 2018 answer (d): cotton, silk, saltpetre (for gunpowder) and opium, the 1750s mix. India exported finished goods, so any option saying "raw" cotton is false (teacher's F on (a)).

Why it sticks: "raw" is the red-flag word; saltpetre goes bang.

PYQ 11 · Prelims 2019: New-World plants (slides 48 and 49)

Memory hook

Only Tobacco travels in both lists.

Two lists, one overlap.

Decode: New-World set in Prelims 2019 answer (a) = tobacco, cocoa, rubber. Portuguese gifts = tobacco, red chillies, papaya, pineapple, guava. Overlap = tobacco; cocoa and rubber came in the British period. Cotton is the Old-World (Bengal) crop, so it is never an American plant.

Why it sticks: read the question's verb: "domesticated in the New World" versus "brought by the Portuguese".

PYQ 14 · Prelims 2022: Gajapati, Albuquerque 09-15, Madras (slides 53 to 55)

Memory hook

The Gajapati elephant is long gone.

"Gaja (elephant) of the 15th century cannot give land to 1605 Dutch."

Decode: Prelims 2022 answer (b) 2 and 3: (2) Albuquerque 09-15 took Goa in 1510 (true); (3) Madraspatanam from the Hindu Nayakas in 1639, Vijayanagara 1336-1646 (true, "purchased" in the handout); (1) Dutch land from the Gajapati dynasty of Odisha is false, since the dynasty had declined long before the Dutch (1605 onwards).

Why it sticks: "2 + 3 = B"; statement 1 is the elephant that no longer exists.

Memory hooks: chapter in one breath

Memory hook

The whole chapter as one voyage.

Story: "Ottomans lock the road (1453); the Pope splits the sea (1494); Gama knocks at Calicut (1498); Cabral builds at Cochin (1500) and Albuquerque 09-15 walls it (1503). Almeida 05-09 wins the sea at Diu (1509), Albuquerque 09-15 grabs Goa (1510), Nuno da Cunha 29-38 makes it the capital (1530). Then the Armada falls (1588), the English (1600) and Dutch (1602) charters sail in, Best wins at Swally (1612) so Jahangir 05-27 signs Surat (1613), and the Danes tag along (1616). Roe gets liberal rights (1618), the Presidencies rise S-M-B-C, and the Charter of 1661 turns the Company into a State. The French last of all at Pondicherry (1674)."

Decode: 1453 Ottoman blockade; 1494 Tordesillas; 1498 Calicut; 1500 Cochin factory; 1503 Fort Manuel; 1509 Diu; 1510 Goa; 1530 capital to Goa; 1588 Armada; 1600 EIC; 1602 VOC; 1612 Swally; 1613 Surat farman; 1616 Danes; 1618 Roe's farman and Surat Presidency; Presidencies Surat, Madras, Bombay, Calcutta; 1661 Charles II 60-85's Charter; 1674 Pondicherry.

Why it sticks: one breath, one direction: from Europe's closed road to the Company that became a State.

Chapter 9: Carnatic Wars (1746–63) (36)open chapter

0 · How to read this chapter

Memory hook

BAM-M-S: who the EIC conquered, in order.

"Bam! Bam! M-M-S!"

Decode: Bengal (Plassey 1757), Awadh (Buxar 1764), Mysore (4 Anglo-Mysore wars), Marathas (3 Anglo-Maratha wars), Sikhs (2 Anglo-Sikh wars).

Why it sticks: the war counts fall 4-3-2 like a countdown. (The board writes the Mysore wars as [1766-69, 80-84, 90-92, 99] but Chapter 7 gives the first as 1767-69; follow Chapter 7 for that year.)

Memory hook

BAH, Ma-Ja-Si-Fa-Ro, Raja-Tr-My: the three classes of States.

Decode: SS (Successor States) = BAH = Bengal, Awadh, Hyderabad. Rebel States = Ma-Ja-Si-Fa-Ro (sing it like do-re-mi-fa-so) = Marathas, Jats, Sikhs, Farukhabad, Rohilkhand. AAs (Already Autonomous) = Rajputs, Travancore, Mysore.

Why it sticks: group sizes 3, 5, 3.

1 · How did a trading company become a State?

Memory hook

Flip to 100.

63 + 37 = 100, and 78 + 22 = 100.

Decode: learn only British India's 63% territory, 78% population; the 565 Princely States are whatever completes 100 (37%, 22%).

Why it sticks: the company that "RULED" (not just "controlled") took the bigger half of both.

1.1 The 1661 Charter

Memory hook

FTWJ: the 1661 Charter of Charles II 60-85.

"Fat Tigers Wage Justice."

Decode: Forts (build them), Troops (command them), War and peace (make them), Justice (civil and criminal jurisdiction over its settlements in India). Then 1676 = the right to mint its own money.

Why it sticks: a tiger with forts, troops and courts is already a mini-State; in 1676 it also prints cash.

1.2 Two kinds of wars: for trade and for territory

Memory hook

Defend at Carnatic, Expand at Suvali.

Decode: wars for trade come in two flavours. Defensive (protect present trade) = Carnatic Wars. Offensive (expand trade) = Battle of Suvali 1612.

Why it sticks: C = Cover what you have; S = Spread to get more.

Memory hook

GA: the two triggers of wars for territory.

"Greedy Governor, Angry Army." Builders: "Clive 57-67 Wanted Fresh Districts."
