Decode: G = a personally ambitious Governor or Governor-General (Britain far away, communication slow). A = the EIC Army pushing for war (budget, prestige, promotions). Builders: Clive 57-67 (Governor of Bengal Presidency, 1757-60 and 1765-67), Richard Wellesley 98-05, Francis Hastings 13-23, Dalhousie 48-56.

Army cycle: overhype threat, expand, new border, new threat, overhype again.

2 · Wars for trade: the Anglo-Mughal War (1686-90)

Memory hook

Two Childs, two places.

"Josiah sits in London; John fights from Bombay."

Decode: Josiah Child = Chairman of the Court of Directors, London (the desk). John Child = Governor of Bombay and "General" of all EIC forces in India (the battlefield).

Why it sticks: the war is named after them, so Child's War = the chairman's order plus the general's army.

Memory hook

86, 90, 91: pay once, then pay small.

Decode: the Anglo-Mughal War starts in 1686 (EIC seizes Hugli, where its first Bengal factory stood in 1651). By 1690 the Company surrenders; Feb 1690 Aurangzeb 58-07 pardons it for Rs 1.5 lakh. 1691 Farman (technically a Parwana from the Subahdar of Bengal) = old privileges back plus duty-free Bengal trade for Rs 3,000 a year.

Why it sticks: Rs 1.5 lakh once to be forgiven, then Rs 3,000 a year to trade free.

2.1 Why did Aurangzeb 58-07 not abolish the Company and expel the British?

Memory hook

RMEB: why Aurangzeb 58-07 kept the Company.

"Ryot makes, Merchant sells, EIC ships, Banker lends."

Decode: ryots and artisans produce; Indian merchants sell to the EIC; the EIC exports and brings in forex/gold; bankers earn interest on loans to the Company.

Why it sticks: everyone in the chain eats, so nobody cuts the chain. The duty the EIC saves buys more Indian goods, so more exports.

3 · The Carnatic Wars (1746-48, 49-54, 56-63): why the French, and why three wars

Memory hook

Dupleix 42-54: "42 to 54, not here in 56."

Decode: Dupleix 42-54 = Governor-General of the French EIC, 1742-54, the template for later empire builders. He was recalled in 1754, so he is absent from the 3rd Carnatic War (1756-63).

Why it sticks: his 12 years end two years before the 3rd war begins.

Memory hook

Odd wars international, even war local; French open, British close.

Decode: wars 1 and 3 = IR (Britain vs France, from global wars); war 2 = R vs R (factionalism). Royal armies only in the 3rd. Result "3/2 = 1.5": French won the 1st war and phase 1 of the 2nd; British won phase 2 of the 2nd and the 3rd.

Why it sticks: the French win the opening, the British win the ending.

3.1 Reasons for the Carnatic Wars

Memory hook

Global war first, Indian war follows.

Decode: War of Austrian Succession 1740-48 gives the 1st CW 1746-48 (both end in 48). Seven Years' Global War 1756-63 gives the 3rd CW 1756-63 (identical years, 7 years each).

Why it sticks: the 3rd war is a carbon copy of the Seven Years' War; the 1st starts 6 years after AWoS begins.

Memory hook

2-5-7 years, and A-P-P treaties.

Decode: war lengths grow: 1st 1746-48 (2 years), 2nd 1749-54 (5 years), 3rd 1756-63 (7 years). Treaties in order: Aix-La-Chapelle 1748, Pondicherry 1754, Paris 1763.

Why it sticks: each treaty year is the war's last year (48, 54, 63).

4.2 Events during the war

Memory hook

7 + 3 = 1,000 beats 10,000.

"Nawab Sent, French Smashed."

Decode: about 700 French-trained Indian sepoys + 300 French soldiers = 1,000 men destroyed the Nawab's 10,000 cavalry, on the north bank of the Adyar (1746). The Nawab (Anwaruddin 44-49) sent the cavalry, not Dupleix 42-54; Dupleix 42-54 refused to hand over Fort St George.

Why it sticks: odds of 1 to 10, and the Nawab (N) is the one who Needed the fort.

4.3 Why the French won: three advantages of European warfare

Memory hook

ADC: the French Adyar edge.

"The French general's ADC."

Decode: Artillery (light, mobile cannon; cf. Babur 26-30's small bronze cannons), Discipline (infantry squares facing every direction), Cash (regular salaries, so no desertion).

Why it sticks: an ADC stands beside the commander; these three stood beside every European army.

4.4 Consequence: the Treaty of Aix-La-Chapelle (1748)

Memory hook

BI-FA: who wanted what in 1748.

"Britain wants India, France wants the Americas: BI-FA."

Decode: France won in India but Britain won in the Americas, so the Treaty of Aix-La-Chapelle (1748) swapped back possessions: Madras returned to the EIC. Results: no change in territory; European infantry far greater than Indian cavalry; Dupleix 42-54 "KAT gaya".

Why it sticks: "Aix" sounds like "aches", which is what Dupleix 42-54 felt.

5.0 The succession disputes that triggered the war

Memory hook

CM for France, the other two for Britain.

"French picks become CMs."

Decode: French candidates = Chanda Sahib 49-52 (Carnatic) and Muzaffar Jung 50-51 (Hyderabad). English candidates = Md Ali 52-95 (Carnatic) and Nasir Jung 48-50 (Hyderabad). Order: French = (1) moved first, English = (2) reacted.

Why it sticks: the teacher's own hooks (Moon and Paris; MuzaFFrance) cover C and M; whoever is left is British.

Memory hook

MWP: British on top, French at the bottom, battle in the middle.

Decode: down the coast, north to south: Madras (HQ of the EIC), Wandiwash (3rd CW battle), Pondicherry (HQ of the French EIC). Arcot sits inland as capital of Carnatic.

Why it sticks: the handout's line, "where the French were washed away", puts Wandiwash between the two HQs.

5.1 Phase 1: French victory (1749-51)

Memory hook

Three deaths, three years: 49, 50, 51.

Decode: 1749 Ambur: Anwaruddin 44-49 killed, Chanda Sahib 49-52 crowned at Arcot. Dec 1750: Nasir Jung 48-50 killed, Muzaffar Jung 50-51 declared Nizam. Feb 1751, Lakkireddipalli Pass: Muzaffar killed by the Afghan Nawab of Kurnool (also killed); Bussy installs Salabat Jung 51-62.

Why it sticks: one ruler falls each year, and the Hyderabad ladder reads Nasir, Muzaffar, Salabat (N, M, S).

The gains from Phase 1

Memory hook

Bussy's hand: five Hyderabad gains.

Decode: Thumb = Resident (Bussy at the Nizam's court). Index finger = a French army (Hyderabad pays). Middle finger (the tall one) = Northern Sarkars as payment, revenues kept by Bussy. Ring finger = cash, 2,00,000 pounds. Little finger = personal jagirs in Masulipatnam and Yanam.

Why it sticks: the ring finger is the money finger. Bonus: Bussy's terror gave Andhra the word "boochi" (ghost).

Memory hook

Krishna to Kumari; 80 villages, 1 lakh.

Decode: Dupleix 42-54 declared himself Nawab of everything from the River Krishna down to Cape Comorin (= Carnatic), with Chanda Sahib 49-52 as Deputy Nawab of Arcot. His personal jagir: Villianallur near Pondicherry, 80 villages, Rs 1 lakh a year (about 300 times that today = 3 crore).

Why it sticks: picture a map triangle, Krishna at the top, Kanya Kumari at the tip; "80 gaon, ek lakh".

Memory hook

SART: Dupleix 42-54's four-step model.

"Smart Armies Take Rule."

Decode: Succession dispute = the opening (R vs R). Army stationed, and the State pays ("free of cost domination"). Territory taken as payment. Resident in the king's court = puppet State, indirect rule.

Why it sticks: each step needs the one before it, like a ladder to the throne.

Memory hook

F then B: Founded, then Built.

Decode: the Mains question splits in two. French Founded the model = 2nd Carnatic War (the four steps). British Built (implemented) it = future topics (Plassey, Buxar, Subsidiary Alliance).

Why it sticks: the alphabet does it, F before B.

Memory hook

D-C-H: Dupleix 42-54 Did it, Clive 57-67 Copied, Hastings Honed.

Decode: Prelims 1996 asks the first European to meddle in Indian princes' quarrels for territory: Dupleix 42-54. Then Clive 57-67, then Warren Hastings 73-85. Albuquerque 09-15 (Goa 1510) = direct conquest, an earlier era.

Why it sticks: the codes themselves rise, 42, 57, 73, so the smallest is the first.

5.2 Phase 2: the EIC bounces back

Memory hook

Arcot 1751: 200 + 300 = 500 men, 53 days.

Decode: Dupleix 42-54 obsessed over Trichinopoly and left Arcot unguarded. Clive 57-67 took it from Madras with about 200 Europeans + 300 sepoys = 500, then held it through a 53-day counter-siege. Paris refused help (to honour the 1748 treaty and keep America); by 1754 France had lost the 2nd CW.

Why it sticks: "five hundred men, fifty-three days": 5-0-0 then 5-3.

5.3 The Treaty of Pondicherry (1754)

Memory hook

BAN: what France kept in Hyderabad (Treaty of Pondicherry 1754).

"French influence stays, French clauses BAN the British from Hyderabad."

Decode: Bussy as agent, Army (French army in Hyderabad), Northern Sarkars with the French. Clause 2: French factories in Pondicherry and Carnatic retained. Clause 3: Md Ali 52-95 becomes Nawab of Carnatic = EIC influence there.

Why it sticks: France smiles in clause 1, Britain smiles in clause 3, a middle path. Also: Dupleix 42-54 recalled, replaced by Godeheu, and died poor in 1763.

Memory hook

Chanda Sahib 49-52 fled to Tanjore and lost his head; 74 = 74.

Decode: Chanda Sahib 49-52 escaped to Tanjore and was beheaded by Pratap Singh 39-63, the Maratha Raja of Thanjavur (an old rival). Maratha rule there began in 1674 when Venkoji (Ekoji), half-brother of Shivaji 74-80, conquered it.

Why it sticks: the 74 in 1674 is the same 74 that starts Shivaji 74-80's code.

6.1 Reason

Memory hook

Royals only in the 3rd: "Lally vs Coote, Bussy vs Clive 57-67".

Decode: Royal (State) leaders: Count de Lally (France) vs Eyre Coote (Britain). Company leaders: Bussy (French EIC) vs Clive 57-67 (EIC). The line-up is [EIC + Britain] vs [French EIC + France].

Why it sticks: royal armies joined in the 3rd Carnatic War 1756-63 ONLY; the two L and C pairs run crossed, Lally-Coote, Bussy-Clive.

6.2 Why did France lose the 3rd Carnatic War (1756-63)?

Memory hook

60, 61, 63: Wandiwash washes, Pondicherry falls, Paris signs.

Decode: 22 January 1760 Wandiwash (Eyre Coote beats Lally, decisive); 1761 Siege of Pondicherry (HQ since 1701, Fort Louis); 1763 Treaty of Paris.

Why it sticks: 60, 61, then a gap year, 63; "where the French were washed away".

Memory hook

B-C-K-W: match the battle to its two sides (Prelims 1995).

"Q for Buxar, Sikh for Chilli, Khadda for Kharda, W for French."

Decode: Buxar (1764) = EIC vs Mir Qasim 60-63, not Mir Jafar 57-60. Chilianwala (1849) = Dalhousie 48-56 vs the Sikhs. Kharda (1795) = Nizam vs the Marathas. Wandiwash (1760) = French vs EIC, the correct pair.

Why it sticks: Chilli is hot like a Sikh; Kharda (kharaa, "standing") = the Nizam stands against the Marathas.

Memory hook

LMNT: why France lost the 3rd war ("Lally's missing element").

Decode: Leadership (Lally, rude, despised by officers and sepoys; Dupleix 42-54 not here). Money (soldiers unpaid for months; British capitalism mature, French feudalism ended only in 1789). Navy (Britain dominated the Coromandel coast). Tactical mistake (Bussy left the Northern Sarkars for Lally; Clive 57-67 took them in 1759).

Why it sticks: four letters for four reasons, and the "element" that is missing is a good commander.

6.3 Why did the EIC win the Carnatic Wars (1746-48, 49-54, 56-63)?

Memory hook

LMNT plus one = the five reasons the EIC won all 3 wars.

Decode: the four LMNT reasons (Leadership, Money, Navy, Tactical mistakes) plus the extra one: France starved Dupleix 42-54 of reinforcements to preserve its 1748 gains. Finance contrast: the EIC = private joint-stock firm with deep capital (and Plassey 1757 wealth); the French EIC = a State PSU with weak finances.

Why it sticks: 6.2 has 4 (Wandiwash, 3rd war), 6.3 has 5 (all 3 wars); private beats PSU.

6.4 The Treaty of Paris (1763)

Memory hook

PM KYC: 3 east, 1 west, 1 Bengal.

Decode: Pondicherry, Karaikal, Yanam = east coast (3). Mahe = the only west coast post (Malabar). Chandernagore = Bengal. Five posts in all.

Why it sticks: "M for Malabar", "C for Chandernagore, the Bengal one"; the three east-coast letters P, K, Y are what is left. France was also barred from a strong army or fortified bases.

Memory hook

P-D-F: "Please Don't Forget" the order the EIC beat its rivals.

Decode: Portuguese by 1612, Dutch by the 1650s, French by 1763, leaving the British EIC the monopoly over India's international trade. Also: the French EIC was abolished by France in 1769, six years after Paris.

Why it sticks: the dates climb 12, 50s, 63 in the same order as the letters.

6.5 Link with Plassey, and what came later

Memory hook

98, 99, 01, 05: the South's Subsidiary Alliance order.

"Hyderabad Made Calls to Travancore": H-M-C-T.

Decode: Hyderabad 1798 (1st Indian state to sign a Subsidiary Alliance), Mysore 1799, Carnatic annexed 1801, Travancore 1805. Earlier, the Treaty of Hyderabad 1766: the Northern Sarkars ceded to the EIC for Rs 7 lakh a year and protection against the Marathas and Mysore.

Why it sticks: "3rd CW + 3.5 decades": 1763 plus about 35 years is 1798.

Memory hooks: chapter in one breath

Memory hook

The Carnatic story of "Dupleix 42-54 and the Five Hundred".

A quarrel in Austria (1740-48) lights India: at Adyar 1746, 1,000 French-trained men crush 10,000 cavalry. Aix 1748 hands Madras back, and Dupleix 42-54 "aches".

So he plays kingmaker (SART): Ambur 1749, three deaths in 49, 50, 51, and Bussy's five-finger gains.

Then 500 men under Clive 57-67 take unguarded Arcot (1751) and hold 53 days; Paris will not help, and Pondicherry 1754 ends the 2nd war with Dupleix 42-54 recalled.

Seven Years' War 1756-63: Lally vs Coote; Wandiwash 22 Jan 1760 washes the French away, Pondicherry falls 1761, Paris 1763 leaves only PM KYC.

1769: the French EIC is abolished. 1798: Hyderabad signs the first Subsidiary Alliance.

Decode: Adyar 1746, Aix 1748, Ambur 1749, Arcot 1751, Pondicherry 1754, Wandiwash 1760, Paris 1763: A-A-A-A-P-W-P in order.

Chapter 10: Battle of Plassey 1757 & Battle of Buxar 1764 (39)open chapter

0 · The chapter on one board

Memory hook

Company 65, Crown 58.

Decode: Company State = 1765 to 1858 (begins with ToA 1765). Crown Colony = 1858 to 26 Jan 1950 OR 15 Aug 1947. The Company State comes in by Diplomacy and War.

Why it sticks: the digits flip, 65 then 58.

Memory hook

"Delhi Rots, Regions Rise, Arrivals Establish": D-R-A-E.

Decode: Decline of the Mughal State, Rise of Regional States, Advent of Europeans, Establishment of the Company State.

Why it sticks: each step makes room for the next.

1 · Plassey marks the beginning of conquest

Memory hook

Plassey plunders, Allahabad legalises.

Decode: extracting wealth = PLUNDER = ATTACK + LOOT, 1757-65, illegal. In 1765 the EIC became FM (Diwan) of Bengal with a legal right to Bengal's wealth, so the loot was legalised.

Why it sticks: Plassey = Plunder (both P); the 1765 treaty is the lawyer that signs it off.

2.1 The four Farmans that strengthened the EIC

Memory hook

S-S-D-D: the four Farmans (1613, 1618, 1691, 1717).

"Surat, Spread, Duty-free, Doctor."

Decode: 1613 Jahangir 05-27: factory at Surat (after Suvali 1612). 1618 Jahangir 05-27: factories spread across the Mughal empire plus liberal trading rights (Thomas Roe). 1691 Subahdar of Bengal (Aurangzeb 58-07's approval): duty-free trade in Bengal for Rs 3,000 a year. 1717 Farrukhsiyar 13-19: "Magna Carta of the Company", won by the doctor, Dr William Hamilton.

Why it sticks: the first two are Jahangir 05-27's, the last two are the 91 and the 17.

Memory hook

Liberal trading rights: duty down, paper down, ports up.

Decode: lower export duties, less paperwork, more ports.

Why it sticks: two arrows down (cost), one up (reach).

2.2 What the 1717 Farman gave, and what the Nawab refused

Memory hook

MQK 17-27's answers: "Yes-but, No, Rent-only".

Decode: the 1717 Farman gave three rights. Duty-free trade (Deccan, Gujarat, Bengal) = allowed, but no Dastak misuse for officials' private trade. Royal Mint = refused (hurt sovereign authority and the commission on gold to sikka). 38 villages around Calcutta = rent allowed, NOT purchase.

Why it sticks: Yes-but, No, Half: the order matches the Farman's order.

3.1 Private trade and what a Dastak was

Memory hook

Fifty in 1750.

Decode: by 1750 private trade by EIC officials was worth 1,00,000 pounds a year, about 50 times their salaries. So the motive to come to India was private trade, not salary. A Dastak = an EIC officer's permit certifying goods as the EIC's so they leave duty-free; misuse = misdeclaring private goods. Cornwallis 86-93 curbed it (raised salaries).

Why it sticks: the year and the multiple share the same 50.

3.2 Who was violating whose law?

Memory hook

Intruder, Friend, Cheat: whose law was broken?

Decode: Interloper (intruder) = non-EIC British merchant trading with India, breaks the Royal Charter (British law), court cases in Britain. Free merchant (friend, an ex-interloper) = sells goods to the EIC inside India, legal; the Rs 100 profit is shared. Dastak misuser (cheat) = EIC official, breaks Indian law (customs duty evasion).

Why it sticks: I, F, D = Intruder, Friend, Dishonest; British law, none, Indian law.

4.1 Why Alivardi Khan 40-56 tolerated the EIC's provocations

Memory hook

G-H-T: how to fortify.

Decode: fill Gaps in the walls, raise the Height, increase the Thickness. The EIC did this at Fort William from 1755, without Alivardi Khan 40-56's permission, because 60% of its trade was Bengal and "Adyar 1746 tells us the Nawab won't help" (ATMANIRBHAR).

Why it sticks: Get Higher, Thicker walls.

4.2 Why Siraj 56-57 did not tolerate them

Memory hook

K, C, A: Siraj 56-57's three moves in alphabetical order.

Decode: Kasimbazar factory seized first, then Calcutta captured, then renamed Alinagar. His triggers: stopped Dastak misuse, the British sheltered Krishna Ballabh (for money), and new fortifications.

Why it sticks: "K-C-A" sounds like KCA, one quick tag: Kasimbazar first, Calcutta next, Alinagar last.

4.3 The Black Hole Tragedy (1756): fact versus propaganda

Memory hook

1-2-3 against 30-40: the Black Hole numbers.

"Holwell counted 146 and 123; historians say 30 to 40."

Decode: J.Z. Holwell claimed 146 locked in, 123 dead (the dead number reads 1-2-3). Modern historians: about 30 to 40 died. Used as propaganda to justify the EIC's military response against Siraj 56-57.

Why it sticks: the inflated death figure is a counting rhyme, 1-2-3, against a real figure of 30 to 40.

Memory hook

60 up, 21 down, 01 again, 40 moved.

Decode: 1760 Holwell builds the monument. 1821 it had fallen into ruin and was demolished. 1901 Curzon 99-05 builds a marble replica. 1940 Netaji Bose's agitation; it is shifted (not demolished) to the graveyard of St. John's Church, Calcutta.

Why it sticks: four verbs in order: build, fall, rebuild, move.

5.1 Clive 57-67 recaptures Calcutta and neutralises the French

Memory hook

Clive 57-67's 1757 chain: "Calcutta, Alinagar, Hugli, Chandarnagar".

Decode: Clive 57-67 comes from Madras with the Royal military and recaptures Calcutta; the Treaty of Alinagar restores all EIC privileges; he destroys the French-used Hugli port and beats the French at Chandarnagar (a French settlement since 1690), because the Seven Years' War was on; then comes the Plassey conspiracy.

Why it sticks: first he takes back the house, signs the paper, then clears the French out of the neighbourhood.

5.2 Plassey, 23 June 1757: conspiracy, not a battle

Memory hook

Bakshi = Bag + Binoculars; Plassey 57 + 7 = Buxar 64.

Decode: Mir Jafar 57-60 was the Bakshi: war paymaster (bag) and head of military intelligence (binoculars), so the real commander. He made a deal with Clive 57-67; at Plassey (23 June 1757) the bulk of the army did not fight; Siraj 56-57 fled and was beheaded on Miran's orders. The EIC's first real military victory came seven years later at Buxar (1764).

Why it sticks: whoever holds the bag and the binoculars decides who wins.

Memory hook

Prelims 2004: "Lodi 26, Marathas 61, Tipu 82-99 4th".

Decode: Ibrahim Lodi lost the First Panipat (1526) to Babur 26-30; in the Third Panipat (1761) Abdali beat the Marathas. Tipu 82-99 died in the 4th Anglo-Mysore War (1798-99, Srirangapatna), not the 3rd. Only statement 3 (Mir Jafar 57-60's conspiracy) is true: (b).

Why it sticks: Tipu 82-99's code ends in 99, and 99 belongs to the 4th war.

6.1 The cash

Memory hook

Everything starts with 2.

Decode: 2,75,000 pounds to the Royal military's soldiers; 2,34,000 pounds to Clive 57-67 (plus a jagir of 35,000 pounds a year); Rs 2.25 crore to the EIC; and 2.5 million pounds as political gifts over 1757-65. The first three came immediately after Plassey.

Why it sticks: only Clive 57-67's jagir (35,000) breaks the "2" pattern, and it is the only yearly payment.

6.2 Beyond the cash: Bengal's economy and polity, the EIC's trade structure and the EIC army

Memory hook

TSP and ICGA: what Bengal's treasury paid for.

Decode: Indian goods (exported), Chinese goods (Tea, Silk, Porcelain = TSP, "teaspoon", sold in Europe), Gold shipped to Britain, a large Army. Before 1757 gold flowed in; after, gold flowed out = Drain.

Why it sticks: a teaspoon stirs the Bengal pot.

Memory hook

PTAP: "Pay The Army, Puppet!" (four effects of Plassey).

Decode: Private trade (now domestic too, through Gomastas, untaxed). Trade structure changed (the Drain begins). Army: a large standing army paid from the plunder. Puppet change: Mir Jafar 57-60 replaced by his son-in-law Mir Qasim 60-63.

Why it sticks: it reads like an order a Company officer might bark.

6.3 The Nawabs of Bengal: the succession

Memory hook

Baton relay: each Nawab's end year is the next one's start year.

Decode: MQK 17-27, Shuja-ud-din 27-39, Sarfaraz Khan (1739-40), Alivardi Khan 40-56, Siraj-ud-Daula 56-57, Mir Jafar 57-60, Mir Qasim 60-63, Mir Jafar 57-60 again 1763-65, then Najm-ud-Daula (1765): the Nizamat as a puppet.

Why it sticks: 27, 39, 40, 56, 57, 60, 63, 65: pass the baton with no gap.

Memory hook

Jafar-Qasim-Jafar sandwich.

Decode: Mir Jafar 57-60 (puppet after Plassey), then his son-in-law Mir Qasim 60-63 (the filling: shifted to Patna, militarised, ended Dastak abuse, then rebelled), then Mir Jafar 57-60 brought back 1763-65.

Why it sticks: the filling is the only one who fights back.

7.1 Why did Mir Qasim 60-63 rebel?

Memory hook

MMM: Mir Qasim 60-63's three moves.

"Move, Money, Merchants."

Decode: Move the base from Murshidabad to Bihar (Patna) to build an army quietly. Money: punish zamindars who underreported revenue. Merchants: abolish all internal duties on domestic trade (like 0 GST), levelling the field against EIC officials' untaxed Gomasta trade. Result: defeated in Bihar; Mir Jafar 57-60 brought back (1763-65); Qasim fled to Awadh.

Why it sticks: each M answers a need: space, funds, support.

7.2 Why Shah Alam II 60-06 was in Awadh

Memory hook

54 blind, 59 dead, 61 defeat: why Shah Alam II 60-06 sat in Awadh.

"Fifty-four, eyes no more; fifty-nine, the Emperor dies; sixty-one, his raid on Bengal is done."

Decode: Wazir Imad-ul-Mulk (also Gaddar-ul-Mulk) blinded Ahmad Shah Bahadur 48-54 in 1754 and murdered Alamgir II 54-59 in 1759. Shah Alam II 60-06 fled Delhi, attacked Bengal in 1761 and lost to the EIC, then stayed in Awadh with Shuja 54-75 as his Wazir.

Why it sticks: two Emperors' fates in the 50s, then the survivor's attempt in 61.

7.3 The battle and after

Memory hook

Three on one side, one Munroe: Buxar 1764.

Decode: Indian side = Awadh (Shuja 54-75, the only State) + the Mughal Emperor (Shah Alam II 60-06, "as an individual") + Mir Qasim 60-63 ("as an individual"). Other side = EIC + Bengal under Hector Munroe (Clive 57-67 was in London). Shuja lost patience and attacked the fort against Qasim's advice; Munroe's final charge turned the tables.

Why it sticks: patience snaps, the charge lands; a close fight means the EIC was only "one of many" kingdoms.

7.4 The teacher's question: why was the EIC able to build an empire?

Memory hook

MANY vs ONE: why India fell and China did not (politically).

Decode: India had multiple States, not united (each wanted to be an empire), so economic and military resources were not united; say "Indian kings were not united", never "Indians". China was weak but one State: the Manchu dynasty, till 1911.

Why it sticks: many kings = many doors; one State = one door.

8 · Treaty of Allahabad 1765 (ToA 1765)

Memory hook

PPP: Paper, Power, Pay: the three parts of ToA 1765.

Decode: signed by Clive 57-67 (EIC), Shah Alam II 60-06 and Shuja 54-75. @ Emperor = Paper (promises: Rs 26 lakh, an army for Delhi). @ Bengal = Power (the Diwani). @ Awadh = Pay (Rs 50 lakh, plus a buffer). Clive 57-67 also bought EIC shares before the terms were public: the "richest man of Europe".

Why it sticks: Emperor, Bengal, Awadh = Paper, Power, Pay, in order.

8.1 @ The Emperor

Memory hook

Emperor's clauses: two promises on paper, one real thing.

Decode: Rs 26 lakh annual tribute (never paid); an army to recapture Delhi from the Rohilla Afghans, who held it since 1761 (never supplied); Allahabad and Kora, taken from Awadh, as the Emperor's seat (real).

Why it sticks: money and army are words; only the land actually moved.

Memory hook

PAT: the EIC dresses as a Mansabdar.

Decode: Post (mansab = the finance ministership, the Diwani of Bengal), Army (a Mansabdar maintains an army for the king = the promise of help), Tribute (a Vatan Jagir holder pays tribute = the Rs 26 lakh). So Bengal is "LIKE" a Vatan Jagir of the EIC.

Why it sticks: the Emperor gives a pat on the back; the EIC gets the real Bengal.

8.2.1 Dual Government (1765-72)

Memory hook

Nazim guards, Diwan counts, Bakshi pays and spies.

Decode: Nazim/Subadar = military commander + law and order. Diwan = "CM + FM": civil administration and finance. Bakshi = military intelligence + war paymaster. Under Akbar 56-05 they reported to the centre, not to each other (checks and balances); by 1717 in Bengal they all reported to the Nawab.

Why it sticks: three jobs, three verbs: guard, count, pay-and-spy.

Memory hook

Diwani = Dhan, Nizamat = Niyam.

Decode: Diwani (Dhan, money) to the EIC: revenue administration, treasury, civil administration and civil justice over Bengal + Bihar + Orissa. Nizamat (Niyam, rule) to the Nawab: law and order, police, criminal justice (the military in practice with the EIC since 1757). Md Reza Khan = Naib Nazim and also the EIC's Diwan: he sits in both boxes.

Why it sticks: money went to the Company, rules stayed with the Nawab: "two bosses, one administration", 1765-72.

Memory hook

Take the Dhan, leave the Dard.

Decode: why the EIC did not annex Bengal: a company works for profit, not to rule; annexation brings cost and responsibility; Plassey already made it militarily safe; Buxar gave the legal right over Bengal's finances; ruling is a tension; it lacked experience of ruling. So it took the Diwani (money) and left the Nizamat (headache).

Why it sticks: Dhan = money, Dard = pain.

8.2.2 Policy of Indirect Rule, the 1770 famine and Direct Rule

Memory hook

PRC: the three steps of Indirect Rule ("Puppet, Resident, Remote control").

Decode: (a) the Nawab is a Puppet; (b) a British Resident sits in his court; (c) the Resident Controls him. It is a 3-step process that takes years: begun in Bengal at ToA 1765, complete by 1772.

Why it sticks: PRC reads like a remote-control (RC) unit.

Memory hook

Famine = Nature AND Human; Bengal 1770 = HHN.

Decode: nature (drought or floods) lifts food prices; a famine needs the human factor to fail too. In 1770 (about 1 crore dead, one-third of Bengal): High revenue demand with harsh collection, Hoarding unchecked, No relief (the EIC said relief was the Nawab's job; the Nawab lacked resources).

Why it sticks: AND is written large on the board: both factors must fail together.

Memory hook

65, 70, 72, 73: Diwani, famine, bankrupt, Hastings.

"'65 gets the Diwani, '70 the famine strikes, '72 the Dual ends and the EIC is bankrupt, '73 Hastings rules directly."

Decode: 1765 ToA, Dual Government begins; 1770 Great Bengal Famine; 1772 Nizamat taken over, Direct Rule, EIC bankrupt (both its incomes, trade and land revenue, collapsed); W. Hastings 73-85 carries Direct Rule.

Why it sticks: the Dual Government lasts 65 to 72, seven years, and Hastings's code starts the very next year.

8.3 @ Awadh

Memory hook

Awadh's four clauses "PBKD", and 26 against 50.

Decode: Shuja 54-75 Pays Rs 50 lakh; Benaras is separated (formally annexed by the EIC in 1775); Kora and Allahabad go to the Emperor; Duty-free trade for the EIC (Dastak misuse spreads). Contrast: the Emperor's Rs 26 lakh was a promise the EIC never paid; Awadh's Rs 50 lakh was actually paid to the EIC.

Why it sticks: the bigger figure is the one that really moved.

Memory hook

Clive 57-67 plants, Hastings hedges.

Decode: Ring Fence = a buffer around the core (broken glass, barbed wire, a thorn hedge: all guard the core). In 1765 Bengal is the core and Awadh the buffer; PoRF = military alliance with neighbours. Clive 57-67 only planted the seed; the match-the-following answer is PoRF = W. Hastings 73-85.

Why it sticks: seed vs hedge: the gardener who plants is not the one who fences.

Memory hook

RAMS in both, "Trimmed" vs "Finished": mini SA 1765 vs formal SA 1801.

Decode: both have Resident, Army of the EIC stationed, Military alliance, Subsidy (all ticked). Difference: in 1765 Awadh's own army was reduced, not disbanded and external functions were legally not taken; in 1801 Awadh's army was disbanded and its foreign policy taken over. Trap: the first Subsidiary Alliance was Hyderabad 1798, not Awadh.

Why it sticks: RAMS butt heads in both; mini trims the army, formal finishes it.

8.4 Clive 57-67's empire toolkit: born in one treaty

Memory hook

One treaty, three tools: 1 for Bengal, 2 for Awadh.

Decode: Clive 57-67's toolkit in ToA 1765: Indirect Rule in Bengal's Nizamat (1); Ring Fence and mini Subsidiary Alliance with Awadh (2). All three ticked by the teacher; Wellesley 98-05 later only refined them.

Why it sticks: I-R-M = "I Rule, Mine": Indirect rule, Ring fence, Mini SA.

9 · Chronology box: 18th-century battles

Memory hook

South, East, South, East: Ambur, Plassey, Wandiwash, Buxar.

Decode: Adyar 1746 (the teacher's fifth, first), Ambur 1749 (South), Plassey 1757 (East), Wandiwash 1760 (South), Buxar 1764 (East). Gaps: 3, 8, 3, 4 years.

Why it sticks: after Adyar the battles zigzag, Ambur (Carnatic), Plassey (Bengal), Wandiwash (Carnatic), Buxar (Bihar, in the East).

Memory hooks: chapter in one breath

Memory hook

The story of "The Doctor's Farman to the Company State".

A doctor cures an Emperor and the 1717 Farman is born (Magna Carta); officers abuse the Dastak and the Nawab's revenue bleeds.

Siraj 56-57 stops the cheating, takes Kasimbazar, Calcutta (Alinagar), and the Black Hole story is born.

Clive 57-67 returns, signs Alinagar, clears the French, and with Mir Jafar 57-60, the Bakshi with bag and binoculars, wins Plassey (23 June 1757) without a real fight: plunder begins (all those 2s), the Drain begins (TSP, gold, army).

Mir Qasim 60-63 (Move, Money, Merchants) revolts, joins Shuja 54-75 and Shah Alam II 60-06, and Hector Munroe wins Buxar (1764), seven years after Plassey.

ToA 1765 pays out Paper, Power, Pay: Diwani to the EIC, Nizamat to the Nawab, Awadh as buffer.

Then '70 famine, '72 bankrupt, '73 Hastings: Direct Rule, and the Company State stands.

Decode: 1717, 1756-57, 1757, 1764, 1765, 1770, 1772.

Chapter 11: Subsidiary Alliance of Wellesley 98-05 (1798–1805) (54)open chapter

0 · What the name means: Subsidiary, or Subordinate, Alliance

Memory hook

Amritsar is flat, Hyderabad is a slide.

Alliance = LEVEL line. Subordinate = SLOPE.

Decode: Alliance = between equals, equal rights (Treaty of Amritsar 1809, Sikh and EIC, on one level). Subordinate = one party's rights less respected (1798, EIC on top, Hyderabad below). Subsidiary = Subordinate.

Why it sticks: you can picture a flat table (Amritsar) and a playground slide (Hyderabad).

1 · Who were the Wellesleys?

Memory hook

RAH! The three brothers.

Richard Rules, Arthur Army, Henry Handles (R-A-H).

Decode: Richard Wellesley 98-05 = Governor General of Bengal, the empire builder. Arthur = military commander, but Royal Army (33rd Regiment of Foot), NOT an EIC officer. Henry = administration and diplomacy of the EIC.

Why it sticks: the cheer "RAH!" lists them in order of rank.

Memory hook

Fort William teaches the "3 L-C" subjects.

Eighteen-hundred, Calcutta, Wellesley 98-05's school: Languages, Laws, Customs for the new civil servants.

Decode: Fort William College = Calcutta, 1800, founder Wellesley 98-05. Purpose (Prelims 2020 and 2021) = train British civil servants in Indian Languages, Laws and Customs before district postings. Founder pair asked in Prelims 2018.

Why it sticks: "Languages, Laws, Customs" is the purpose; no mention of sepoys or English-teaching.

1.1 Arthur Wellesley: a Royal Army officer, not an EIC officer

Memory hook

Arthur's three marches.

"Ninety-nine, Tipu 82-99's town; Oh-three, Assaye's crown; Oh-eight, the Frenchman's frown."

Decode: 1799 = Siege of Seringapatam (4th Anglo-Mysore War 1798-99, Tipu 82-99 falls). 1803 = Battle of Assaye (2nd Anglo-Maratha War 1803-05). 1808 to 1815 = fights Napoleon's army. Regiment: 33rd Foot, a Royal (not EIC) unit.

1.2 Two European armies in India (3rd Carnatic War 1756–63 till 1858)

Memory hook

Company pays its own; the King lends and sends the bill.

Decode: Company's Army = the EIC's own private military, paid by the EIC, mostly Indian sepoys, always British senior officers. King's Army (Royal Military) = British State regiments sent to support the EIC; the EIC has to reimburse the British State. Both side by side from the 3rd Carnatic War 1756-63 till 1858.

Why it sticks: "King lends, Company pays" also reminds you the British State shares responsibility.

2.1 Context of Pitt's India Act 1784 (the teacher's board)

Memory hook

Seventy, seventy-two, seventy-three, eighty-four.

"Seventy GBF, seventy-two broke, seventy-three leash, eighty-four lock."

Decode: GBF 1770 starts the board chain. 1772 = EIC bankrupt, seeks British State help. 1773 = loan, but supervision via the Regulating Act. Then more wars (1st Anglo-Maratha 1775-82 plus 2nd Anglo-Mysore 1780-84, high war expenditure). 1784 = Pitt's India Act locks conquest.

Why it sticks: the rhyme "broke, leash, lock" tells the whole money story.

2.2 The ban and the reason for it

Memory hook

Defend yes, declare no: ask "CD or BC".

Decode: Under Pitt's India Act 1784 the GG could start an offensive war only with the express command of the Court of Directors (CD) or the Board of Control (BC). Defence needed no permission. Reason for the ban = high war expenditure.

Why it sticks: "CD or BC" is the two-door permission slip.

Memory hook

Three, one, two, one: the war-bill count.

"Car-Bux-My-Ma" = 3-1-2-1.

Decode: 3 Carnatic Wars (1746-63); 1 Buxar (1764); 2 Anglo-Mysore Wars (1767-69 and 1780-84); 1 First Anglo-Maratha War (1775-82). These drained Company finances, so Parliament chose diplomacy: Policy of Indirect Rule plus balance of power.

2.3 The Napoleon threat: there, but not real

Memory hook

Egypt knocks, the Navy blocks.

"Ninety-eight: Napoleon at Egypt's gate, Tipu 82-99 waves; the Navy bars the sea. THERE, not REAL."

Decode: 1798 = Napoleon attacks Egypt (aim: Middle East, hurt British trade of the Arabian Sea); contact with Tipu 82-99; fear = Napoleon-Mysore alliance or attack on British India. Reality: British Navy superior, so the French could not reach India. Teacher's PRELIMS line: "Threat was THERE but not REAL."

Memory hook

CBS: control with no Cost, Burden or Symbol.

Decode: SA = "control without annexation" = political domination without (C) the cost, (B) the administrative burden and (S) the symbolism of full annexation. The teacher's handwritten "w/o" sits before each.

Why it sticks: CBS is a TV channel; Wellesley 98-05 got control "on air" without paying the bill.

Memory hook

Warren Way-before, Francis Following, Wellesley 98-05 in the middle.

Decode: Warren Hastings 73-85 = pre-Napoleon (wrong). Francis Hastings 13-23 (Lord Hastings) = post-Napoleon (wrong). Wellesley 98-05 overlaps Napoleon's offensive phase (1796-1812) = Prelims 1999 answer (c).

Why it sticks: W for Way-before and Wellesley 98-05 is the one who stands W-ithin the war years; both Hastings are off the clock.

Memory hook

"Danger there" is TRUE.

Decode: Prelims 2018 and 2021 option (b) "Napoleonic danger was a feature of the SA" is TRUE: the danger was not real, but it was there. Do not tick it as false.

3 · Two roots of the SA: Dupleix 42-54 and ToA 1765

Memory hook

D-B-H versus C-A-A: French first, British second.

Decode: French root = Dupleix 42-54, Bussy, Hyderabad, 1751 (Salabat Jung 51-62 as Nizam; French force paid from the Northern Sarkars). British root = Clive 57-67, Allahabad (ToA 1765), Awadh (EIC army paid by Awadh subsidy, Nawab's army reduced, Resident). Years: 51 comes before 65.

Why it sticks: "DBH" = Dupleix-Bussy-Hyderabad; "CAA" = Clive-Allahabad-Awadh, two three-letter tags.

3.1 Wellesley 98-05's step: from a device to a policy

Memory hook

Idea: Dupleix 42-54 and Clive 57-67. Policy: Wellesley 98-05. "W-F-A".

Decode: Wellesley 98-05 did not invent the SA but initiated it as a policy through W-F-A: (W)ritten treaty, (F)oreign Policy taken over, (A)rmy disbanded, applied State by State. Statement "invented" = wrong; "initiated the Policy" = right.

Why it sticks: "Invented" has no W-F-A, "initiated" does.

4.0 The teacher's board: how the SA splits sovereignty

Memory hook

EIC keeps the "DC-FA" cupboard; the king keeps the home shelf.

Decode: External functions taken by the EIC = D-C-FA = Defence, Communications, Foreign Affairs. Domestic functions with the Indian king = Home, Finance, Transport, Agri, Health (and symbolic sovereignty). Subsidy = Def BD, size decided by the EIC.

Why it sticks: "outside the house" (external) goes to the foreigner; "inside the house" (domestic) stays with the king.

4.0b The same board, redrawn at the start of Lecture 15 (Treaty of SA)

Memory hook

Resident, Alliance, then the cupboard split: R-A + "DC-FA vs home".

Decode: Board order = (R) Br Resident, (A) military Alliance, then sovereignty: symbolic sovereignty with the Indian king; external functions D-C-FA with the EIC; domestic functions (Fin, Home, Admin, Health, infra) with the king; the subsidy is the Def BD and is "for military, not income".

Why it sticks: you can redraw the whole treaty from the first two letters R-A plus the "DC-FA" cupboard you already know.

4.1 What the EIC got (5 terms)

Memory hook

P-R-C: two shown, one hidden.

Decode: Indirect rule = (1) Puppet Nawab, (2) British Resident, (3) Control. The SA treaty shows P and R openly; control is "promised away" by the non-interference clause (the teacher's crossed-out circled 3). Term 2: the ruler's army can serve British India too. Term 4 = Def BD. Term 5: money goes to the State, decisions to the EIC.

Memory hook

"Raj Always Disarms, Squeezes, Fixes-foreign".

Decode: What the EIC got, in order: (R) Resident at court, (A) military Alliance, (D) State army Disbanded and replaced, (S) annual Subsidy, (F) Foreign Policy taken over. Count = 5. The subsidy was earmarked for the army, not surplus income.

Why it sticks: R-A-D-S-F reads in the table's 1-2-3-4-5 order.

4.2 What the Indian ruler got (2 terms)

Memory hook

Two crumbs for the ruler, five keys for the EIC.

Decode: Ruler got 2 = (P) Protection against neighbours and domestic threats, (N) Non-interference in succession, Diwani, Nizamat (minus military). EIC took 5 levers. The non-interference promise was routinely broken via the Resident: creeping PoIR through the back door.

Why it sticks: "P-N" sounds like "pen": the promise lived only on paper.

4.3 Why the EIC took Foreign Policy: the legal logic

Memory hook

The Nizam pays the guard; the Company holds the whistle.

Decode: The army was paid from the State's subsidy, so the ruler could claim the right to deploy it (the loophole). Taking Foreign Policy = holding war and peace, so the right to deploy legally rested with the EIC (loophole closed).

Why it sticks: whoever holds the whistle sends the guard out, whoever pays only pays.

Memory hook

Mini = two Missing items.

Decode: ToA 1765 at Awadh had the EIC army at Awadh's cost but was missing (1) disbanding of Awadh's own army and (2) takeover of Foreign Policy. So it was only a "mini" SA; the full package came in 1801.

Memory hook

I-R-E-A-L: "I REALly lost control".

Decode: PoSA = (I) PoIR informally + (R) PoRF (Ring Fence) + (E) EIC military replacing the State's military + (A) Annual subsidy + (L) Loss of Foreign Policy.

Why it sticks: the five letters spell REAL with an I in front, the ruler's lament.

4.4 The PYQ on SA features (Prelims 2018 and Prelims 2021)

Memory hook

A-N-P true, F for False.

Decode: Prelims 2018 and 2021 "NOT apply to SA": (A) large standing Army, (N) Napoleonic danger, (P) British Paramountcy are true. (F) Fixed income for the Company is the false one, because the subsidy was earmarked for the army.

5 · The vicious chain: consequences of the Subsidiary Alliance for Indian States

Memory hook

Unlucky Farmers Dread Tax Men Taking Land.

Decode, in link order: (U) Unemployment of disbanded soldiers, (F) Free army for the EIC, (D) Drain of Wealth through high rising subsidy, (T) Tax hikes, (M) Maladministration charge, (T) Territory in lieu of cash, (L) Lost alliance-forming capacity. Count = 7.

Why it sticks: a farmer's story, and the sentence has exactly seven words.

5.1 Links 1 and 2: unemployment and the EIC's free army

Memory hook

Mansabdar = Military ROLE, not "positions".

Decode: The handout says "positions"; the teacher struck it out and wrote "MILITARY ROLE". Use the teacher's wording. Same two M's: Mansabdar, Military.

Memory hook

King pays the sepoys, Company plays the war.

Decode: Who pays for the EIC army in an Indian State? The Indian State/king, so the army is free of cost to the EIC and every SA increases it. Sepoys are recruited from outside the host State (the teacher's DnR) so they have no loyalty to the local Nawab.

5.2 Links 3 and 4: Drain of Wealth and tax hikes

Memory hook

S-T-A-R of unrest.

Decode: (S) Subsidy rises, (T) Taxes raised, (A) Anger (peasant resentment, rulers unpopular), (R) Revolts (the teacher's "Peasant Revolts"). Drain of Wealth is the leak at the start: the same money could have built the State.

Why it sticks: each letter is one step up the staircase to unrest.

5.3 Links 5 and 6: maladministration and territory in lieu of cash

Memory hook

Awadh: 01 half, 56 whole. Hyderabad: 53 Berar.

Decode: 1801 = half of Awadh taken as permanent subsidy payment; 1856 = Awadh annexed entirely, Nawab Wajid Ali Shah accused of maladministration (the starkest example). 1853 = Hyderabad made to give Berar. Teacher: "Subsidy = TOOL of empire building."

Why it sticks: "01" looks like "one half", and half becomes whole by 56.

6.1 Why some rulers walked in willingly

Memory hook

I Eat Banana Daily, Sir.

Decode: Five reasons rulers walked in: (I) Internal revolts protection, (E) External threats protection, (B) Better than annexation, (D) royal Dignity preserved, (S) Succession disputes and neighbours (the EIC made help conditional on signing).

Why it sticks: five words, five arguments, in table order.

Memory hook

Fifth reason = Factions.

Decode: Of the five reasons (I-E-B-D-S), the one the teacher tags is S = Succession disputes = FACTIONALISM. Others: internal revolts, external threats, better than annexation, dignity.

Why it sticks: "Fifth" and "Factions" both start with F.

6.2 The ten signatories (1798–1818)

Memory hook

Mysore lost the war, so signed the form.

Decode: Hyderabad (1798) signed first by choice, for protection. Mysore (1799) signed only after defeat in the 4th Anglo-Mysore War, Tipu 82-99 killed at Seringapatam. The teacher's lessons: one common centre, and "Don't lose wars".

Why it sticks: "lose war, sign form" is two beats.

Memory hook

Ten fingers, twenty years: H-T-H.

Decode: 10 SAs in 20 years (1798 to 1818). (H) Hyderabad 1798 = first; (T) Travancore 1805 = last under Wellesley 98-05 before recall; (H) Holkar 1818 = last of all. Split 8 + 2.

Why it sticks: "H-T-H" starts and ends with H, Travancore is the middle step.

Memory hook

Strike nine, write ten; strike seven, write eight.

Decode: Teacher's two corrections: 9 to 10 SAs; 7 to 8 under Wellesley 98-05 (8 + 2 = 10, the last two under Francis Hastings 13-23).

Why it sticks: both strikes move up by one (9 to 10, 7 to 8).

Memory hook

Clive 57-67, Wellesley 98-05 | Hastings, Dalhousie 48-56: two and two.

Decode: Empire builders on the teacher's list: 18th c = Clive 57-67 and Wellesley 98-05; 19th c = F. Hastings 13-23 and Dalhousie 48-56.

Why it sticks: "C-W then H-D" is two pairs, one per century.

Memory hook

Hungry Mice Ate Good Pizza, Sharing Juicy Tomatoes; Big Hunger.

Decode, in signing order: Hyderabad 1798, Mysore 1799, Awadh 1801, Gaikwad June 1802, Peshwa Dec 1802, Scindia 1803, Jats-Rajputs-Bundelas 1803-05 (group entry, no. 7), Travancore 1805; then Bhonsle 1816 and Holkar 1818. Count = 10: the first eight under Wellesley 98-05, the last two under Francis Hastings 13-23.

Why it sticks: ten words, two sentences: eight mice, then "Big Hunger" for the last two.

Memory hook

Six names, S-S-S-A-W-S.

Decode, in order: (S) Sadat 22-39, (S) Safdar Jung 39-53, (S) Shuja 54-75, (A) Asaf-ud-Dowla 75-97, (W) Wazir Ali [1797-98], (S) Sadat II Ali Khan [1798+]. Each reign starts about where the last ended (39, 53/54, 75, 97, 98).

Why it sticks: three S's open the line, then A for Asaf, W for the wobbling Wazir, and an S to close it.

Memory hook

One oval, two halves: 01 | 56, then 36 and 77.

Decode: Awadh oval = left half 1801 (Ceded Districts, NWP 1836), right half 1856 (annexed); 1877 = both merged.

Why it sticks: read the four numbers across the oval: 01, 56, 36, 77.

Memory hook

Uncle pays 76, nephew goes; Henry takes D-G-R.

Decode: 1798 = Sadat Ali Khan II offers Rs 76 lakh annual subsidy so that John Shore 93-98 removes his nephew Wazir Ali from the Nawabship (Shore obliges). 1801 = default; Wellesley 98-05 sends his brother Henry, who imposes the full SA. Half of Awadh annexed = D-G-R: Doab, Gorakhpur, Rohilkhand (revenue twice Rs 76 lakh; later Ceded Districts, NWP 1836).

Why it sticks: one family quarrel, one price tag, three places.

Memory hook

74 gave, 01 took: R for Rohilkhand's Remnant = Rampur.

Decode: 1774 = most of Rohilkhand annexed by Awadh with EIC help (ToA 1765 alliance). The remaining small part became the autonomous State of Rampur, under Awadh legally and from 1801 under the EIC.

Memory hook

Hit, Bolt, Sign, Scream, War.

Decode: Yashwantrao Holkar defeats (Hit) Bajirao II 96-18, avenging his brother's death; Bajirao II 96-18 flees (Bolt) and signs (Sign) the Treaty of Bassein, December 1802, to regain the Peshwaship; Scindia and Bhonsle scream "sold the Confederacy"; the 2nd Anglo-Maratha War (1803-05) follows.

Why it sticks: five short verbs run in time order.

Memory hook

Three quarrel at home, two fight in the field: P-B-G versus S-H.

Decode: Factionalism = Peshwa, Bhonsle, Gaikwad (P-B-G: Bassein, Nagpur, Cambay). War = Scindia (2nd Anglo-Maratha War) and Holkar (3rd Anglo-Maratha War, Mandsaur 1818).

Why it sticks: "Please Bring Gifts" settles a family quarrel; "Shoot, Hit" is war.

Memory hook

Cab, Bus, Snake, Mandi: match the treaty.

Decode: Gaikwad took a Cab = Cambay (June 1802). Peshwa took a Bus = Bassein (December 1802). Bhonsle met a Nag(snake) = Nagpur (1816, Appa Sahib). Holkar went to the Mandi = Mandsaur (1818).

Why it sticks: four letters C, B, N, M match four rulers G, P, B, H in signing order.

Memory hook

Bassein twice: 1739 and 1802.

Decode: Treaty of Bassein (1739) = Marathas and the Portuguese (Bassein plus Salsette under Peshwa control). Treaty of Bassein (1802) = Peshwa and the EIC (an SA).

Why it sticks: same bus stop, two journeys: first Marathas win, then Peshwa signs away.

Memory hook

Tipu 82-99's code ends where the war ends: 99.

Decode: 4th Anglo-Mysore War is printed "1798-99" in some places and "1799" in others, but the fall of Seringapatam and the death of Tipu 82-99 are 1799 in every version. "Siege" and "Battle" of Seringapatam are the same event.

7 · Direct annexations and the recall of Wellesley 98-05

Memory hook

Carnatic: Record says "mal-admin", ledger says "loans".

Decode: Carnatic 1801: recorded reason = maladministration (six years after Md Ali 52-95 died in 1795); teacher's context = the Nawab owed many loans to the EIC and its officers (financial maladmin).

Why it sticks: a ledger is the book that explains the record.

Memory hook

"But still, wars": the refrain of the board.

Decode: Loan (Regulating Act 1773) but still wars; Pitt's India Act 1784 but still wars; Wellesley 98-05's two wars (Mysore 1799, 2nd Anglo-Maratha 1803-05) end in recall for high war expenditure. Winner and loser both pay.

Why it sticks: two "but still"s, two wars, one recall.

Memory hook

Wellesley 98-05's MAC, then B-O and S-G-D.

Decode: Direct annexations: (M) Mysore territory 1799, (A) half of Awadh 1801, (C) Carnatic 1801 (maladministration, six years after Md Ali 52-95 died in 1795). 1803-05 war: Bhonsle loses Orissa (B-O); Scindia loses Gujarat and all north of the Yamuna including Delhi (S-G-D).

Why it sticks: "MAC" is one gadget, and 95 + 6 = 01.

Memory hook

The money circle: Bill, Ban, Trick, Bill, Boot.

Decode: High war expenditure (Bill) leads to Pitt's India Act 1784 (Ban); Wellesley 98-05 gets round it with the Napoleon threat and the SA (Trick); his wars again run up high war expenditure (Bill); he is recalled to London (Boot).

Why it sticks: the chapter starts and ends on the same word, money.

8.1 PYQ 1: Prelims 1999, the Governor General who kept the flag flying (slides 11 to 14)

Memory hook

Seventy-three, eighty-six | ninety-eight, thirteen.

Decode: The teacher's two brackets: 1700s = Warren Hastings 73-85 and Cornwallis 86-93 (both wrong); 1800s = Wellesley 98-05 (correct, starts 1798) and Francis Hastings 13-23 (wrong, after 1815).

Why it sticks: two pairs of two-digit starts: 73, 86, 98, 13.

8.2 PYQ 2 and PYQ 3: "Which does NOT apply to the Subsidiary Alliance" (Prelims 2018, Prelims 2021; slides 15 to 18)

Memory hook

Three T's from the Bali board, one F for the fixed income: B-T-L-F.

Decode: (a) T with Bali and Free-of-army; (b) T with Threat (real or unreal); (d) T with Loss of D, C, FA; (c) F = fixed income for the Company, which the earmarked subsidy is not.

Why it sticks: Bali, Threat, Loss are the three true tags; "F" is the odd one out.

8.3 PYQ 4: Prelims 2018, institution and founder pairs (slides 19 to 21)

Memory hook

Two F's, one true: Jones F, Arthur F, Madarsa-Hastings T.

Decode: Pair 1 (Sanskrit College Banaras, Jones) is F: Jonathan Duncan 1791. Pair 3 (Fort William, Arthur) is F: Richard Wellesley 98-05, 1800. Only pair 2 (Calcutta Madarsa, Warren Hastings 73-85) is right, so (b) Only 2.

Why it sticks: the two circled F's are the two wrong names, Jones and Arthur.

Memory hook

Duncan in Banaras, Richard in Calcutta.

Decode: Jonathan Duncan = Sanskrit College Banaras (1791); Richard Wellesley 98-05 = Fort William College, Calcutta (1800).

Why it sticks: two founders, two cities, two dates: 1791 and 1800.

8.4 PYQ 5: Prelims 2020, why Wellesley 98-05 established Fort William College (slides 22 and 23)

Memory hook

Fort William = Furnish administrators: (d), not (a), (b) or (c).

Decode: (a) the Board asked = no, Wellesley 98-05's own idea; (b) oriental learning = the Asiatic Society (William Jones from 1784); (c) jobs for William Carey = no, he joined later; (d) train British civilians for administration = yes.

Why it sticks: "F-W-F-A": Fort William Furnishes Administrators.

Memory hooks: chapter in one breath

Memory hook

The Wellesley 98-05 story in ten beats.

Seventy-two the Company broke, seventy-three the leash, eighty-four Pitt locked the war-gate.

Ninety-eight: Richard Wellesley 98-05 shouts "Napoleon at Egypt, Tipu 82-99 beside!" The threat was there, not real, but the gate swings open.

Hyderabad signs first (98), Mysore falls (99), Awadh gives half (01), Cambay and Bassein follow (02), Scindia (03), Travancore the last of his own (05).

Raj Always Disarms, Squeezes, Fixes-foreign: the king pays, the Company plays.

The bill climbs again, Wellesley 98-05 is recalled, and Bhonsle (1816) and Holkar (1818) finish the ten under Francis Hastings 13-23.

Decode: 1772 bankrupt, 1773 Regulating Act, 1784 Pitt's India Act ban; 1798 Napoleon in Egypt; ten SAs in order (Hyderabad 1798, Mysore 1799, Awadh 1801, Gaikwad/Cambay 1802, Peshwa/Bassein 1802, Scindia 1803, Jats-Rajputs-Bundelas 1803-05, Travancore 1805, Bhonsle/Nagpur 1816, Holkar/Mandsaur 1818); recall for high war expenditure.

Chapter 12: Anglo-Mysore Wars (1766–99) + Travancore + Carnatic (34)open chapter

0 · The chapter on one board

Memory hook

F-W-I: why Indian States lost.

Decode: Factionalism, War defeats (fear of neighbours: Hyderabad), Internal revolts (Travancore). Root cause: multiple States, motive = Territory, so rivalry; the EIC's edge = better leadership and a better army.

Why it sticks: "F-W-I" sounds like "FWI, for what?" The answer is "for why they lost".

Memory hook

57, 64, 67, 75: gaps 7, 3, 8.

Decode: 1757 Plassey, 1764 Buxar (7 years later), 1767 1st Anglo-Mysore (3 years later), 1775 1st Anglo-Maratha (8 years later); then 1844-45 1st Anglo-Sikh. Each family has four, four, three and two entries: Mysore 4 (1767-1799), Maratha 3 (1775-1819), Sikh 2 (1844-49).

Why it sticks: "7-3-8" is a phone number; dial it and the first four wars ring.

Memory hook

Win, Tie, Lose, Lose: two "nothing lost", two "everything lost".

Decode: 1st 1767-69 Mysore won (Treaty of Madras), 2nd 1780-84 tie (Treaty of Mangalore), 3rd 1790-92 lost (Treaty of Seringapatam), 4th 1799 lost. The gaps between the starts shrink: 13, 10, 9 years (1767, 1780, 1790, 1799).

Why it sticks: the EIC's pace quickens, 13 then 10 then 9.

Memory hook

Return, Return, Lose: the three treaties.

Decode: Madras 1769 (both sides return territories), Mangalore 1784 (both sides return territories, status quo), Seringapatam 1792 (Mysore loses half the kingdom, 3 crore, two sons). Madras and Mangalore start with M and mirror each other; Seringapatam is the only one that hurts.

Why it sticks: two handshakes, then one hammer.

1.1 Military Fiscalism

Memory hook

Military is the Mission, Fiscalism is the Fuel.

Decode: Military = GOAL (a stronger army), Fiscalism = TOOL (maximise State revenue to pay for it). Same model the EIC used from 1765. Mysore added a navy and French arms for trade concessions.

Why it sticks: M-M and F-F: Mission and Military, Fuel and Fiscalism.

Memory hook

T-F-D: the teacher's three threats, and "Ma-Hy-Tr-Ca" the four anxious neighbours.

Decode: Trade (Tipu 82-99's government company versus the EIC), France (Br vs Fr rivalry; Hyder Ali 61-82 and Tipu 82-99 allied with France), Dream (VN 2.0, unclear). Neighbours who feared Mysore: Marathas, Hyderabad, Travancore, Carnatic.

Why it sticks: say it as "Ma, Hi, Tra, Ca": Ma says "Hi" to Tra-Ca.

1.2 Mercantilist trading State

Memory hook

PCS embargo; FTE embassies.

Decode: Tipu 82-99's embargo covered Pepper, Cardamom, Sandalwood (late 1780s). His delegations went to France, Turkey, Egypt. He also made the Mauludi lunisolar calendar (Prophet's birth era) to replace the Hijri.

Why it sticks: an aspirant's "PCS" for goods, "FTE" for faraway friends.

Memory hook

Prelims 2001: "T for Tipu 82-99, T for Turkey, T for Trade company."

Decode: the ruler who sent embassies on modern lines is Tipu 82-99; the codes on the options: Haider Ali 61-82, Mir Qasim 60-63, Shah Alam II 60-06.

Why it sticks: the only option with a code that ends in 99 is the one that looked furthest ahead.

Memory hook

Hyder 61-82, Tipu 82-99: the baton passes at 82.

Decode: Hyder Ali 61-82 died of cancer in December 1782; Tipu 82-99 took over and continued the 2nd war. Tipu 82-99's code ends in 99, the year of the 4th war.

Why it sticks: both codes share the number 82, one ends where the other begins.

2.1 Reason

Memory hook

1766: four given, Guntur kept, seven lakh promised.

Decode: 1766 Treaty of Hyderabad: the Northern Sarkars go to the EIC (4 of 5 districts handed over; Guntur was not), the EIC promises 7 lakh rupees a year and military help to Hyderabad against Mysore. The EIC wanted Andhra to join Bengal and Madras.

Why it sticks: Guntur is the fifth finger held back; it comes back to bite in 1780.

Memory hook

"Confederacy = Conspiracy against the Company; Alliance = Allies of the Company."

Decode: Triple Alliance = EIC + Hyderabad + Marathas against Mysore, formed 1766-67 and again 1790. Triple Confederacy (1780-82) = Marathas + Mysore + Hyderabad against the EIC, built by Nana Fadnis. Same three Indian powers, opposite sides.

Why it sticks: C for Confederacy and Company-opponent, A for Alliance and Allied-with-Company.

2.2 Events

Memory hook

35 for the Marathas, 20 for the Nizam: the bigger power, the bigger bribe.

Decode: 1767 Hyder 61-82 pays the Marathas Rs 35 lakh to exit; he gives the Nizam Rs 20 lakh plus the promise of making his son Nawab of Carnatic and a joint attack on Carnatic (Md Ali 52-95's State). 1768 Hyderabad, fearing an EIC counter-invasion, makes peace with the EIC.

Why it sticks: M is bigger than N in the alphabet's second half, and 35 is bigger than 20.

2.3 Result: the Treaty of Madras (1769)

Memory hook

Offensive = Steel 39; Defensive = NATO 49, SEATO 54, Baghdad 55, CENTO 58.

Decode: Offensive alliance (valid even if a member is the aggressor): Pact of Steel 1939, Germany and Italy. Defensive alliance (valid only if a member is the victim): NATO 1949, SEATO 1954, Baghdad Pact 1955 [CENTO 1958]. Pakistan was a member but the aggressor in 1965 and 1971.

Why it sticks: after 49 the years climb 54, 55, 58: steps of 5, 1, 3.

Memory hook

Madras: gates, give back, guard.

Decode: Treaty of Madras 1769: Hyder 61-82 at the gates of Madras; both sides give back territory; a defensive alliance to guard each other against a third party. It was a farce, as 1771 and 1780 showed.

Why it sticks: three G words, in order of events.

3.1 Reason for war

Memory hook

71 refused, 78 France, 79 Mahe, 80 war.

Decode: 1771 Marathas attack Mysore, the EIC refuses its 1769 duty. 1778 France enters the American War (1776-83). 1779 the EIC seizes French Mahe (under Mysore's protection; Hyder 61-82's arms route). 1780 Hyder Ali 61-82 invades Carnatic. The American Revolution: protest 1765-76, war 1776-83.

Why it sticks: four steps up the years 71, 78, 79, 80, the last three almost consecutive.

3.2 Events

Memory hook

Baillie's ranks burn at Pollilur: Sep 1780, iron rockets.

Decode: Pollilur, September 1780: Colonel Baillie's infantry is annihilated by Hyder 61-82's iron-cased rockets; Arcot, the Carnatic capital, had been taken just before. One of the worst defeats ever suffered by a European army in India.

Why it sticks: "rocket" and "Pollilur" both have an "o" and an "l": the rocket lit up the line.

Memory hook

Nana's three: My-Ma-Hy, and Hyderabad's two grievances: Guntur and 7 lakh.

Decode: Triple Confederacy 1780-82: Mysore + Marathas + Hyderabad against the EIC, built by Nana Fadnis (the first Indian statesman to see the EIC's empire ambitions: PRE). Hyderabad joined because the EIC illegally held Guntur and stopped the 7 lakh a year.

Why it sticks: "My Ma Hy": My Ma says Hi to the Nana.

3.3 How the EIC broke it, and the result

Memory hook

G-S: Guntur back, Salbai signed; then Hyder dies, Mangalore ties.

Decode: Warren Hastings 73-85 returns Guntur to Hyderabad and signs Salbai (May 1782) with the Marathas (it ends the 1st Anglo-Maratha War 1775-82). Hyder 61-82 dies, Dec 1782; Tipu 82-99 fights on; Mangalore 1784 returns territories (stalemate).

Why it sticks: 82 for Salbai and for Hyder's death, 84 for the stalemate: "82, 82, 84".

4.1 Reason and events

Memory hook

Travancore trigger, Cornwallis 86-93 chooses, three fronts close.

Decode: 1789 Tipu 82-99 attacks Travancore (a formal EIC ally) → Cornwallis 86-93 declares war → Triple Alliance 1790 (EIC + Marathas + Hyderabad; in 1782-84 the Marathas had stayed out) → three-front pressure → Seringapatam 1792.

Why it sticks: every stage hands the baton to the next: trigger, chooser, closer.

4.2 Result: the Treaty of Seringapatam / Srirangapatnam (1792)

Memory hook

Half, Three, Two: the price of 1792.

Decode: Half the Kingdom (Malabar, Dindigul, Baramahal: M-D-B), 3 crore rupees as a war fine, 2 of Tipu 82-99's sons as hostages.

Why it sticks: "Half, 3, 2" counts down a staircase; "M-D-B" is "Mad Dogs Bite": the three lost pieces.

5.2 Events and result

Memory hook

Prelims 2004: "Lodi 26, Marathas 61, Tipu 82-99 dies in 4th, 99."

Decode: Ibrahim Lodi lost the First Panipat (1526) to Babur 26-30. At the Third Panipat (1761) Abdali beat the Marathas. Tipu 82-99 died in the 4th war (1799), not the 3rd. Only statement 3 (Mir Jafar 57-60's conspiracy at Plassey 1757) is true: (b).

Why it sticks: Tipu 82-99's code ends in 99, and 99 is the 4th war.

Memory hook

Five-year-old king, ninety-nine, SA.

Decode: 1799: the Wodeyars return in Mysore's central core, Krishnaraja III, age 5, under a Subsidiary Alliance (1799); Kanara and Coimbatore go to the EIC (with Hyderabad). Hyderabad fought because of its SA of 1798. The Peshwa (Baji Rao II 96-18) sent no army and refused the SA offer, on Nana Fadnis's advice.

Why it sticks: "5 on the throne, 99 on the calendar".

Memory hook

Tipu 82-99's death chain: Napoleon, Wellesley 98-05, Hyderabad, Arthur, Wodeyar.

Decode: Napoleon letters (the pretext) → Wellesley 98-05 gets approval → Hyderabad must fight (SA 1798) → Arthur Wellesley (Royal regiment) at the siege → Tipu 82-99 dies defending the capital → Wodeyar restored under an SA.

Why it sticks: five names, one story, ending with the old dynasty.

6 · Why did Mysore lose?

Memory hook

Four NOs: no ally, no fleet, no funds, no fortress-safety.

Decode: No stable alliance (and weak outside ally, domestic rivals). No French fleet (France busy in the American War 1776-83, then the Revolutionary Wars 1792-1804; Britain's navy stronger). No money (Military Fiscalism over-extended; 3 crore fine; "BUT"). No safe Seringapatam (surrounded by a three-way coalition in 1789-92 and 1799).

Why it sticks: say "A, F, M, S" as "A Fort Made Sad"; the verdict, "fought well, fought alone".

7 · Dhondia Wagh

Memory hook

99, 00, 01: Dhondia rides, Dhondia falls, the EIC reigns.

Decode: 1799-1800 Dhondia Wagh (ex-prisoner of Tipu 82-99, "King of Two Worlds") raids the Mysore-Maratha borders with soldiers disbanded under the SA; 1800 killed in action by Colonel Arthur Wellesley; 1801 the EIC formally supreme in the south.

Why it sticks: three consecutive two-digit years, rise, fall, rule.

8 · A note on Tipu 82-99's religious policy

Memory hook

Heart gets gifts, edges get force; Coorg is the corridor.

Decode: Heartland (Mysore): Sringeri Sharada Math, the Shankaracharya's blessings, yajnas before wars, temple grants. Conquered lands: Malabar (Nairs and Christians converted; churches and temples destroyed) and Coorg 1785 (Kodavas). Order north to south: Mysore, Coorg, Malabar; Coorg links capital to coast. The King of Coorg joined the EIC in 1790.

Why it sticks: M-C-M from top to bottom, with the middle one as the road between the other two.

Memory hook

Tipu 82-99: two faces, one tactic.

Decode: patronage at home (the heartland) and coercion in conquered Malabar and Coorg lead to the handout's conclusion: secularism was a tactic for domestic stability, not a value. A practice statement that calls his policy "uniformly tolerant" is the wrong one.

Why it sticks: statements that say "uniformly tolerant" are the wrong ones (FYQ statement 3).

9.2 Carnatic Treaty (1801): annexation under Wellesley 98-05

Memory hook

Loan, Collateral, Interest: how the Carnatic Debts worked. Money and Mole: the two charges.

Decode: Loan from private EIC officials at high interest → Jagirs as collateral → the officials collect the land revenue as interest. Charges in 1801: financial maladministration ("Money") and secret contact with Tipu 82-99 ("Mole"). Md Ali 52-95 died 1795; 95 + 6 = 01.

Why it sticks: L-C-I are three steps of a trap; Money and Mole are the two accusations.

10.2 British Resident stationed (1800), Subsidiary Alliance signed (1805)

Memory hook

Travancore's ladder: 95 Friend, 00 Watcher, 05 Master, 09 Rebel.

Decode: 1795 Treaty of Perpetual Friendship (pay for a British force against Tipu 82-99); 1800 a British Resident in the court (the imprint of ToA 1765); 1805 the proper SA, in exchange for crushing the Nair mutiny; 1809 Kundara. Before it: Marthanda Varma 29-58 (Colachel 1741), Rama Varma 58-98 (died 1798); the 1789 attack.

Why it sticks: steps of five (95, 00, 05), then a jump of four to 09.

Memory hook

Pay the army, pay the price.

Decode: the Nair troops mutinied over unpaid salary; the EIC helped crush them; the king paid with sovereignty under the standard SA template: the SA was extracted not at sword-point but for internal-security help.

Why it sticks: the king could not pay his soldiers, so he paid the EIC more.

Memory hook

Marthanda 29-58 builds, Rama 58-98 hedges.

Decode: Marthanda Varma 29-58 built the centralised revenue-based State and beat the Dutch at Colachel (1741); Rama Varma 58-98 kept the EIC alliance as a hedge against Mysore, which triggered Tipu 82-99's 1789 attack. The baton passes at 58.

Why it sticks: the code numbers join: 58 ends one and begins the other.

11 · Velu Thampi Dalawa and the Kundara Proclamation (1809)

Memory hook

Dalawa 02-09, Kundara January 09.

Decode: Velu Thampi Dalawa was PM 1802-09, rose against subsidy demands and interference (a breach of the SA's own promise of non-interference), issued the Kundara Proclamation in January 1809 from Kundara; crushed within months; he died that year.

Why it sticks: his tenure ends the same year he rebels, 09.

12 · Legacy: southern consolidation completed

Memory hook

99, 01, 05, 09: Wodeyar, Carnatic, Travancore, Cochin.

Decode: 1799 Mysore's Wodeyars under an SA; 1801 Carnatic annexed (direct rule); 1805 Travancore SA; 1809 Cochin SA and the Kundara revolt crushed. EIC paramount from the Krishna to Kanyakumari. FYQ chronology: 4th war, Carnatic, Travancore SA, Kundara: 3, 1, 2, 4.

Why it sticks: steps of 2, 4, 4: 99, 01, 05, 09.

Memory hooks: chapter in one breath

Memory hook

The story of "The Rocket, the Fine and the Five-Year-Old King".

Mysore under Hyder 61-82 and Tipu 82-99 runs on Military Fiscalism (Military = Mission, Fiscalism = Fuel) and a trading company, so the EIC and four neighbours (Ma-Hy-Tr-Ca) are anxious.

1766 Malabar goes to Hyder 61-82, the Sarkars to the EIC (Guntur kept); 1767-69 Hyder 61-82 buys off the Marathas (35) and the Nizam (20), and signs Madras 1769 with the EIC at his gates.

1771 the EIC refuses to help, 1779 it seizes Mahe, 1780 Pollilur: Baillie burns to iron rockets; Nana's Triple Confederacy (My-Ma-Hy) falls to Hastings 73-85's Guntur back, Salbai signed; Mangalore 1784, a tie.

1789 Tipu 82-99 hits Travancore, Cornwallis 86-93 rebuilds the Triple Alliance, and Seringapatam 1792 costs Half, 3, 2.

1799 Napoleon letters, Wellesley 98-05, a bound Hyderabad, Arthur Wellesley at the siege: Tipu 82-99 falls; a five-year-old Wodeyar rules under an SA. Mysore lost for four NOs (ally, fleet, funds, fortress).

Dhondia 99-00, then 01 Carnatic (Loan, Collateral, Interest; Money and Mole), 05 Travancore (pay the army, pay the price), 09 Kundara: the EIC from the Krishna to Kanyakumari.

Decode: 1766, 1769, 1780, 1784, 1792, 1799, 1801, 1805, 1809.

Chapter 13: Anglo-Maratha Wars (1775–1819) till 1848 (44)open chapter

0 · The chapter on one board

Memory hook

F-F-B, Won-Lost-Lost: the three wars in one breath.

Decode: reasons: Factionalism (1st, 1775), Factionalism (2nd, 1802), Breach of SA by the EIC (3rd, 1817). Results: Won (Salbai 1782, a military alliance), Lost (Scindhia signs SA), Lost (Peshwaship abolished, Svarajya annexed).

Why it sticks: "F-F-B" sounds like a quick fax; "W-L-L" tells you the EIC won only the first war, on points, and the last two outright.

1 · Why did the Deccan matter so much to the EIC?

Memory hook

C-C-M: coast, cotton, Malabar.

Decode: the Deccan mattered for its large coastline, the Gujarat-China cotton trade, and Maratha control over parts of the Malabar coast. The Marathas and Mysore were the last two powers to check the EIC: 3 + 4 = 7 wars (three Maratha, four Mysore).

Why it sticks: "CCM" is one seaside shop; seven wars is a week.

2.1 What triggered the war?

Memory hook

Murder, Mutiny, Mortgage: 1773, 1774, 1775.

Decode: 1773 Narayan Rao 72-73 murdered, Raghunath Rao 73-74 becomes Peshwa; 1774 the Baarbhai Conspiracy (Nana Fadnis + eleven ministers = twelve, a dozen) removes him; 1775 he mortgages Salsette, Bassein and revenue to the EIC (Treaty of Surat).

Why it sticks: three years, three steps, and the "12" in Baarbhai (Nana plus eleven) is one dozen.

Memory hook

Surat pays in land and money: SB for land, BS for money.

Decode: Treaty of Surat 1775 = Salsette and Bassein (land) + Bharuch and Surat (revenue). Salsette and Bassein are the "S-B" pair; the revenue towns are the "B-S" pair, flipped.

Why it sticks: the second pair is the first pair reversed.

2.2 Warren Hastings 73-85 overturns Surat: the Treaty of Purandhar (1776)

Memory hook

Purandhar 1776, three P-S-F clauses: Peshwa, Salsette, French.

Decode: Peshwa (Madhav Rao II 74-95 recognised, Raghunath Rao 73-74 pensioned off), Salsette and Bharuch to the EIC, French kept out. Negotiated by Warren Hastings 73-85 with Nana Fadnis.

Why it sticks: "PSF" like a bank deposit scheme; Hastings pays off the old claimant.

Memory hook

Two Purandhars: 1665 and 1776.

Decode: the teacher's bracket warns of two treaties at one fort: 1665 (Chapter 4) and 1776 (this chapter, Warren Hastings 73-85 with Nana Fadnis). Both years contain a 6.

Why it sticks: 16-65 and 17-76, the 6 sits in both, so "6 means Purandhar".

Memory hook

Calcutta signs, Bombay hides, Poona flirts with France.

Decode: Warren Hastings 73-85 (Calcutta) signs Purandhar; Bombay keeps sheltering Raghunath Rao 73-74 (factionalism within the EIC); Nana Fadnis (Poona) offers a port to the French; war resumes.

Why it sticks: three cities, three moves, and each one breaks the treaty.

2.3 Events and the Treaty of Salbai (1782)

Memory hook

Wadgaon 79: N-M-T won.

Decode: Battle of Wadgaon (1779), near Pune: Nana Fadnis, Mahadji Scindhia and Tukoji Rao Holkar beat the EIC and Raghunath Rao 73-74. The war then stalled until 1782 while the Marathas were also busy with the 2nd Anglo-Mysore War (1780-84).

Why it sticks: N-M-T is "NMT", a three-man team; 79 then 82, three years of stalemate.

Memory hook

Salbai = A-S-F-R: Alliance, Salsette, French, Return.

Decode: Salbai 1782, signed at Gwalior by Mahadji Scindhia: Alliance (Marathas fight for the EIC against Hyder Ali 61-82 if he keeps the territories), Salsette and Bharuch to the EIC, French excluded, Return of all Maratha territory (plus Madhav Rao II 74-95 recognised, Raghunath Rao 73-74 pensioned).

Why it sticks: "ASFR" sounds like "as fair": the Marathas got their land back and the EIC got an ally.

Memory hook

Triple Confederacy: M-M-H, 1780-82.

Decode: Marathas + Mysore + Hyderabad, an anti-British alliance of 1780-82. It gave two wars: the 1st Anglo-Maratha War (1775-82) and the 2nd Anglo-Mysore War (1780-84). Salbai broke the confederacy by buying the Marathas out.

Why it sticks: M-M-H is a humming sound: three voices, then Salbai silences one.

3 · The inter-war period (1782-1803)

Memory hook

94 dies, 95 suicides, 96 crowned, 1800 the glue goes.

Decode: 1794 Mahadji Scindhia dies; 1795 Madhav Rao II 74-95 commits suicide; 1796 Bajirao II 96-18 becomes Peshwa; 1800 Nana Fadnis dies, the Confederacy's glue. The gap from Salbai (1782) to Bassein (1802) is 21 years.

Why it sticks: four consecutive-ish years, four verbs: dies, suicides, crowned, gone; and 82 plus 21 is 1803, one year after Bassein.

4.1 The trigger: Bassein (December 1802)

Memory hook

V-Y-B: Vithoji's Vengeance Brings Bassein.

Decode: Vithoji Rao Holkar executed on the Peshwa's orders (1801) -> Yashwantrao Holkar defeats the Peshwa and Daulat Rao Scindhia (October 1802) -> Bajirao II 96-18 flees and signs Bassein (December 1802): SA, army of Svarajya to be disbanded.

Why it sticks: Vengeance chain: a stepbrother's blood leads to a Peshwa's flight.

Memory hook

Cambay before Bassein: June, then December.

Decode: the Gaikwad signed the Treaty of Cambay (June 1802) for EIC help in a succession dispute; the Peshwa signed Bassein (December 1802). The Gaikwad is the first signatory of the 1802 pair.

Why it sticks: June comes before December, and Cambay (a small place) signs before Bassein.

4.2 The battlefield: Assaye (1803)

Memory hook

Assaye 1803: Arthur's "toughest", tougher than Napoleon.

Decode: Assaye (1803): Arthur Wellesley's Royal regiment fought with the EIC army against the Marathas. His own verdict: the toughest fight of his career, harder than anything against Napoleon. Arthur is the soldier; Wellesley 98-05 is the Governor General.

Why it sticks: "Arthur at Assaye" starts with A twice; the brother with the code (98-05) sits in Calcutta.

4.3 Consequences

Memory hook

Six masters of the Emperor: Z-S-I-S-M-E.

Decode: Zulfikar Khan, Sayyid Brothers, Irani and Turani group, Safdar Jung 39-54, Marathas, EIC. The sixth master after 1803 is the EIC, through the Resident in Delhi (Policy of Indirect Rule) and Shah Alam II 60-06.

Why it sticks: "ZSISME" reads as "Zis is me": each new master says "this is me" and takes the Emperor.

Memory hook

GPS-BH: the order of the Subsidiary Alliances.

Decode: Gaikwad (June 1802, Cambay), Peshwa (Dec 1802, Bassein), Scindhia (1803/04), then Bhonsle (1816, Treaty of Nagpur; a mini alliance after the 2nd war) and Holkar (1818, Treaty of Mandsaur).

Why it sticks: like a GPS giving directions: five turns, in order, one per Maratha power.

Memory hook

P-S-B-J-W: the five results of the 2nd war.

Decode: Peshwa puppet; Scindhia loses all north of the Yamuna (Delhi, Agra, Gujarat) and signs SA; Bhonsle loses Orissa and accepts a mini alliance; Jats, Rajputs and Bundelas sign SA; Wellesley 98-05 recalled (high war expenditure), Cornwallis 86-93 sent for non-interference.

Why it sticks: "PSBJW" = "Please Send Back Just Wellesley 98-05": the story ends with Wellesley 98-05 recalled.

4.4 The Vellore Mutiny (1806)

Memory hook

Vellore 1806: Barlow's bad dress code.

Decode: under George Barlow (Acting GG, 1805-07): no tilak, no turban; a leather cockade (cross-shaped, cow or pig skin) instead; sepoys rise in Vellore Fort (north Tamil Nadu) in 1806: the "Prelude to 1857".

Why it sticks: the fort, the cockade and the cow; and 1806 comes before 1857.

5 · The Pindaris, Francis Hastings 13-23 and the Policy of Paramountcy (1813-58)

Memory hook

Warren = the first War, Francis = the Final one.

Decode: Warren Hastings 73-85 handled the 1st Anglo-Maratha War (Purandhar 1776, Salbai 1782); Francis Hastings 13-23 handled the 3rd (Pindaris, Paramountcy 1813-58, the Peshwaship abolished).

Why it sticks: W for Warren and War, F for Francis and Final.

Memory hook

Pindaris = sacked soldiers raiding the Sarkars.

Decode: disbanded soldiers of Scindhia and Holkar, raiders of the Northern Sarkars and parts of Madras Presidency; crushed by Francis Hastings 13-23, who then ran the Policy of Paramountcy (1813-58).

Why it sticks: "pink slips" turned into pirates on land.

Memory hook

Paramountcy runs 13 to 58, like the Company State.

Decode: PoP starts 1813 (Francis Hastings 13-23) and runs to 1858. The aim: the EIC supreme; do anything, even annex, to protect its interests.

Why it sticks: 13 is the start year and also the first two digits of the code 13-23; 58 matches the end of Company rule (Chapter 10's "Company 65, Crown 58").

Memory hook

Nagpur 1816: R-A-R, retainers allowed, Arabs and Rohillas recruited.

Decode: Treaty of Nagpur (1816): Appa Sahib signs SA for help over who will be regent; state army to be disbanded, but retainers and police allowed; he secretly hires Arab and Rohilla mercenaries; murders Parsoji; wants to rule independently.

Why it sticks: "RAR" is a roar: the loophole that roars back in 1817.

Memory hook

Same script, third time: Surat, Bassein, Nagpur.

Decode: Raghunath Rao 73-74 (1775, Surat), Bajirao II 96-18 (1802, Bassein), Appa Sahib (1816, Nagpur): each rulers' succession or power dispute made him ask the EIC for help, and each time the EIC gained a foothold.

Why it sticks: three actors, one script: "Help me, EIC!"

6.1 The Battle of Sitabuldi (November 1817)

Memory hook

BAH! The three who revolted in 1817: Bajirao 20-40, Appa, Holkar.

Decode: Bajirao II 96-18, Appa Sahib (Bhonsle of Nagpur) and Holkar united against the EIC in late 1817. Bajirao II 96-18 and Appa Sahib fought with men they recruited themselves (the SA had disbanded the state army); Holkar alone had a proper army (teacher's board).

Why it sticks: "Bah!" is how a rebel spits at the Resident.

Memory hook

Sitabuldi 1817: hills, Residency, Arabs, still lost.

Decode: Appa Sahib as Senapati sent his Arab mercenaries in a surprise, close-quarters attack on the British Residency and the Sitabuldi hills (November 1817); heavily outnumbering the garrison, he still lost. Prelims twist: SA signed in 1816, war in 1817.

Why it sticks: "Sita-buldi", sits on the hills; the Nagpur hills hold, and the SA's loophole is exposed.

6.2 Battles and surrender

Memory hook

Nov, Dec, Jan, June: S-M-K-S.

Decode: Sitabuldi (November 1817, Appa Sahib), Mahidpur (December 1817, Holkar), Koregaon (1 January 1818, Peshwa; Mahar soldiers, Bhima-Koregaon Victory Pillar), Surrender (June 1818, Bajirao II 96-18).

Why it sticks: "N, D, J, J" is a run of months and "S-M-K-S" sounds like "smacks": each battle smacks one Maratha power.

Memory hook

Three battles, three men: Sitabuldi-Appa, Mahidpur-Holkar, Koregaon-Peshwa.

Decode: Bhonsle's regent Appa Sahib lost at Sitabuldi; Holkar at Mahidpur; the Peshwa at Koregaon (small Mahar force against a much larger army). The EIC used its largest force ever: about 1.2 lakh.

Why it sticks: the order of the letters S-M-K matches the order of the battles.

7 · Consequences of the Third Anglo-Maratha War (1817-19)

Memory hook

P-S-A-M-S: five results of the 3rd war, "Pension, Swallow, Oust, Mandsaur, Spare".

Decode: Peshwaship abolished, Bajirao II 96-18 pensioned to Bithur (adopted son Nana Sahib, Kanpur 1857); Svarajya swallowed into the Bombay Presidency; Appa Sahib ousted (puppet replaces him, Bhonsle loses huge territory); Holkar signs SA, Treaty of Mandsaur (1818); Satara and Kolhapur spared.

Why it sticks: five verbs in order, "pension, swallow, oust, sign, spare".

Memory hook

Bithur near Kanpur: the pension that came back in 1857.

Decode: Bajirao II 96-18 pensioned to Bithur, near Kanpur; his adopted son Nana Sahib led the Kanpur revolt in 1857. The handout's figure says "the bloodline tactic backfires" (compare Raghunath Rao 73-74's pension in 1782).

Why it sticks: Bithur and Kanpur both start where a B and a K meet; the pensioner's son returns to the same city.

Memory hook

Satara fell in 1848: Dalhousie 48-56's first year.
