Decode: 1857 falls between 56 and 62, so Canning 56-62 was GG during the Sepoy Mutiny (and the first Viceroy in 1858). Bentinck 28-35 earlier; Lytton 76-80 later; Hardinge 10-16 in the 1900s.

Why it sticks: the revolt is the year in the middle of his code.

Memory hooks: chapter in one breath

Memory hook

The story of "The Company That Lost Its Head, Body and Name".

The trader becomes a ruler after ToA 1765, and the State grows nervous: money (1767), America (1765-83), bankruptcy (1772), famine, Nabobs.

1773: Hastings 73-85 becomes GG with four councillors and a casting vote, Calcutta gets a Crown-chosen Supreme Court, and the Directors must report.

1784: Pitt's Board of Control takes the political side (Double Government 1784-1833, Dual Control 1784-1858), the council shrinks to GG + 2 + CIC, and conquest is "repugnant".

1793: the Crown takes the head (sovereignty, Principal Servants after approval) and Cornwallis 86-93 writes the code; 1813 ends the India trade (Tea, Opium, China stay) and brings missionaries; 1833 makes the GG of India, adds Macaulay, ends all trade and opens the UCS; 1853 gives no term, an exam in 1855, the Budget to Parliament, 6 Legal Members and Halliday as LG.

1857 is the final nail: the GOI Act 1858 hands the body to the Crown (SoS, 15-member India Council, Canning 56-62 the first Viceroy, Paramountcy over 565 princes), the Proclamation promises no annexation, and the Royal Titles Act 1876 makes Victoria Empress at Lytton 76-80's 1877 Darbar.

Decode: 1765, 1773, 1784, 1793, 1813, 1833, 1853, 1858, 1874, 1876-77.

Chapter 18: EIC Administration: Judiciary, Civil Services, Police (45)open chapter

1 · Administration as the machinery of the State

Memory hook

Judge, Collector, Constable: the three pillars.

Decode: Judiciary, Civil Services (the Collector is the face of it), Police (the Constable). Territory plus people is what they rule.

Why it sticks: you meet them in that order in an Indian town: court, collectorate, thana.

2.1 Why judicial reform was so complicated

Memory hook

Wise Writers Hear Lawyers In Languages: the six questions.

Decode: Which law, Written or discretion, Hierarchy of courts, Lawyers allowed, Indians or British judges, Language of courts.

Why it sticks: W-W-H-L-I-L is the order on the slide, and the sentence reads like a court scene.

2.2 The Mughal judicial system in Bengal (before 1772)

Memory hook

Z-A-D: the revenue ladder, bottom to top.

Decode: Zamindar's Kachari (original jurisdiction), then Amil (the Amalguzar, district revenue officer), then Diwan (chief justice of the SDA at Murshidabad). Personal matters go along a separate rung: Pandit or Maulvi in the Diwani Adalat.

Why it sticks: Z to A to D is the alphabet backwards, going up.

Memory hook

Kazi decides, Mufti guides, Faujdar finishes.

Decode: the Kazi pronounces the verdict; the Mufti gives the expert reading of Islamic law; the Faujdar (like today's SP) secures the accused and executes the sentence. Civil courts follow your faith; criminal courts follow the State.

Why it sticks: three jobs, three K-M-F verbs, in the order of a trial.

2.3 The Dual Government period (1765-1772)

Memory hook

One man, two hats, two masters.

Decode: Reza Khan headed the Diwani (for the EIC) and the Nizamat (for the Nawab) during 1765-72; Orientalism meant Indian officials stayed in charge; the EIC only wanted revenue; the result was poor revenue and a collapse of law and order, then the 1770 famine (one-third of Bengal).

Why it sticks: one Indian official was carrying both the Company's and the Nawab's bag.

2.4 Direct Government (1772) and the judicial plan of Hastings 73-85

Memory hook

Civil: DC sits. Criminal: DC watches. Top: Governor presides.

Decode: 1772 Diwani court = British DC is the judge (with Pandits and Maulvis); Nizamat court = Kazi and Mufti judge, DC supervises; SDA and SNA = Governor (GG from 1773) and Council. Reza Khan jailed 72 to 74, then back as head of the SNA.

Why it sticks: sit, watch, preside: three verbs, three levels.

Memory hook

C-M-B: the Supreme Courts, 74, 00, 23.

Decode: Calcutta 1774 (Regulating Act 1773, only for British subjects under British law), Madras 1800, Bombay 1823. A separate track from the Indian courts.

Why it sticks: C, M, B is the order of the three Presidencies; the years climb 74, 00, 23.

Memory hook

75-76, 78-91, 81: the three codes.

Decode: 1775 11 Pandits compile Hindu law, 1776 English Gentoo Code; 1778 Islamic law effort, Arabic Al-Hidayah to Persian to English, 1791 The Hedaya; 1781 Code: written orders compulsory (in Bengal). Path of the Hedaya: Arabic, Persian, English: A-P-E.

Why it sticks: the years climb 75, 76, 78, 81, and only the Hedaya (91) comes late.

2.5 The Cornwallis 86-93 Code of 1793 (judiciary)

Memory hook

Land-lords need Law-lords: why the DC lost the gavel.

Decode: PS1793 made zamindars owners with a sunset clause; a DC who auctions an estate and then judges the owner's appeal has a conflict of interest; so Cornwallis 86-93 made the DC revenue-only (separation of powers, Prelims 2010 answer (c)).

Why it sticks: the new landlords wanted a different man in the judge's chair.

Memory hook

Four divisions, Pat's Mother Cooks Dal: Patna, Murshidabad, Calcutta, Dacca.

Decode: Patna, Murshidabad, Calcutta, Dacca: the four divisions of Bengal, each with one Provincial Court of Appeal (civil) and one Court of Circuit (criminal). Civil ladder: District DA or City Court, then Provincial Court, then SDA (now in Calcutta).

Why it sticks: P-M-C-D in the board's order; four divisions, four courts, four courts.

Memory hook

1781 by the GG can be undone; 1793 by Parliament cannot.

Decode: the Code of 1781 (Hastings 73-85, Bengal only) came from the GG and could be changed by a future GG; the Cornwallis 86-93 Code came through the Charter Act 1793 (British Parliament). The difference is the issuing authority.

Why it sticks: higher the issuer, stronger the law.

Memory hook

Circuit = circle: week 1 district 1, week 2 district 2.

Decode: 4 Courts of Circuit (Patna, Murshidabad, Calcutta, Dacca) rotate through the districts of a division; Nizamat Adalat at district abolished; appeal goes to the Sadar Nizamat Adalat; law = Anglo-Muhammadan until IPC 1860 (about 70 years).

Why it sticks: the court goes round like a wheel, a week in each district.

Memory hook

I-D-M: Intent, Diyat, Mutilation: three loopholes shut.

Decode: Intent (not the weapon) decides murder (mens rea); Diyat (blood money) ends, so relatives cannot pardon (crime = offence against the State); Mutilation ends, replaced by 7 to 14 years of hard labour and rigorous imprisonment.

Why it sticks: the three are the old Islamic rule, then the new British rule: weapon, family, limb became intent, State, jail.

Memory hook

Father of three: C-P-L.

Decode: Cornwallis 86-93 is the father of Civil services, Property dealers and Lawyers. Authoritarian: Indians out of all senior posts; Kazis and Muftis only assistants; Naib Nazim abolished.

Why it sticks: CPL is the rank of a corporal, a small officer with three stripes.

Memory hook

S-F-L: Separation, Fees, Lawyers: why courts clogged.

Decode: Separation of powers (key reason: disputes moved from the Collector's table to the court), Fees abolished (free justice flooded courts; fees back in 1795, two years later), Lawyers (Vakils) plus a rigid ladder of appeals: delay.

Why it sticks: free entry, a long queue and a paid advocate in front of it.

Memory hook

Free in 93, fee back in 95.

Decode: court fees were abolished with the 1793 Code and reimposed in 1795.

Why it sticks: the two numbers differ by 2, and so do the two years.

2.6 Judicial system in Ryotwari areas

Memory hook

Zamindari splits, Ryotwari merges: Munro 14, Elphinstone 19.

Decode: few big landowners (zamindari) means separate judge and collector; many small ryots means the DC judges too. Madras 1814 (on Munro's advice), Bombay 1819 (Elphinstone, a follower of Munro). Gain: faster justice, more Indians below. Loss: one official holds tax, order and judgement (Madras Torture Commission 1855).

Why it sticks: M-14 then B-19, the two Presidencies in age order after Bengal.

2.7 Charter Act 1833 (judicial impact)

Memory hook

59-60-61: C-I-C, Civil, Indian Penal, Criminal.

Decode: CPC 1859, IPC 1860, CrPC 1861, the three codes of the Law Commission (1834, Macaulay) that were drafted by 1837 but implemented only after 1857. Personal laws stayed in personal matters.

Why it sticks: three codes, three years in a row, in the order Civil, Penal, Criminal procedure.

Memory hook

One GG, one law, one Law Member.

Decode: Charter Act 1833: the GG of India is the single legislature for British India; laws apply to Indians and Britishers alike; the new Law Member is Macaulay, who heads the Law Commission 1834.

Why it sticks: one, one, one: the Act is about unity.

2.8 Reforms by Bentinck 28-35 (GG 1828-33; GG of India 1833-35)

Memory hook

Bentinck 28-35's Bin, Persian, North: B-P-N.

Decode: Bin the divisional courts (Provincial Courts of Appeal and Courts of Circuit of Cornwallis 86-93: parking grounds, high expense, pendency); Persian out in 1835 (vernaculars below, English above; Act XXIX of 1837); North-West Provinces get their own Sadar Diwani and Sadar Nizamat Adalats (1801 annexed, 1836 full status).

Why it sticks: the three words in the order of the slide.

Memory hook

S-H-A: Sultanate, Humayun 30-40, Akbar 56-05: how Persian won.

Decode: Sultanate (13th century: introduced), Humayun (Safavid military help against the Suris 1540-55 in return for promoting Persian: boost), Akbar 56-05 with Todar Mal (compulsory for all administrative records). Bentinck 28-35 ended it in 1835.

Why it sticks: S-H-A reads "Shah", a Persian title.

2.9 Indian High Courts Act 1861 and 2.10 the Federal Court (GOI Act 1935)

Memory hook

Three into one: SDA + SNA + SC = Calcutta High Court.

Decode: the Indian High Courts Act 1861 (Act year 61) gave High Courts in Madras, Bombay, Calcutta in 1862 (court year 62); Calcutta merged the Sadar Diwani Adalat, Sadar Nizamat Adalat and Supreme Court. Then Federal Court 1935 Act, working 1937, is the ancestor of the Supreme Court (1950).

Why it sticks: the dual track of Indian courts and the British Supreme Court finally becomes one road.

3.1 Issues in civil services, and what a "service" means

Memory hook

N-U-S-L-A, then C and T: the seven design questions.

Decode: Nomination or exam, UPSC-type body or not, Syllabus (Indian or Western), Location (London or India), Age (low or high), Common exam or separate per service, Training (on the job or institute).

Why it sticks: "Nusla" for the first five, then C and T for Common and Training.

3.3 Training

Memory hook

Fort 1800, Hailey 1806, University 1858: F-H-U.

Decode: Fort William College, Calcutta (1800-06, Wellesley 98-05, Indian laws, customs, languages); Haileybury, London (1806-58); Universities in Britain (from 1858). Before 1800: on the job.

Why it sticks: each college hands over in the year the next one starts: 1800, 1806, 1858.

3.4 Cornwallis 86-93: founder of civil services

Memory hook

Covenant in London = Group A; hired locally = Group B.

Decode: CCS (Covenanted, signed a covenant with the Court of Directors in London, high pay, Europeans only under Cornwallis 86-93, like Group A) versus UCS (Uncovenanted, hired in India by the GG or governors, lower pay, where Indians served, like Group B).

Why it sticks: "covenant" = a signed promise made before the ship sails.

Memory hook

Cornwallis 86-93: pay up, trade down, powers split, seniority rules; merit exam NOT till 1853.

Decode: salaries massively raised; private trade curbed; separation of powers (DC without judicial power, 1793); strict seniority for promotion; and the one thing he did not do: merit-based entry (nomination by the Court of Directors until the Charter Act 1853).

Why it sticks: four yes and one no; the no is the Prelims trap (Statement "he introduced a competitive exam" is false).

3.6 Charter Act 1853: the exam opened to Indians

Memory hook

53-54-55: law, committee, exam; then 64 and 67.

Decode: 1853 Charter Act mandates the exam (Indians eligible); 1854 Macaulay's Select Committee and the Civil Services Commission (London); 1855 first exam; 1864 Satyendranath Tagore first Indian; 1867 four more.

Why it sticks: three years in a row for the system, then the Indians arrive nine years later (64) and three years after that (67).

Memory hook

Age shrinks 23, 22, 21, then jumps to 19.

Decode: 1859 max age 23 (Crown); 1860 22; 1866 21; 1876 19 (implemented 1878). The teacher marks this ladder "not imp"; the point is "to prevent entry of Indians".

Why it sticks: a countdown by one, then a jump of two: 23, 22, 21, 19.

Memory hook

London exam, English Literature, low age: the three locks on the door.

Decode: venue London (travel cost), syllabus English Literature ("Brown Sahibs", source of values), age 18 to 23, kept deliberately low (to prevent Indian entry).

Why it sticks: three locks, three keys the Briton already had.

3.7 The fight for Indianisation (1860 onwards)

Memory hook

Lytton 76-80 lets Loyalists Leap Laterally.

Decode: instead of an exam in India or a higher age, Lytton 76-80 brought lateral entry by nomination (Statutory Civil Services Act, 1878 handout and 1879 slide), only loyal elite Indians, into posts reserved for CCS officers. Teacher: "unimportant act."

Why it sticks: four L words, Lytton 76-80, loyal, lateral, leap.

Memory hook

Two demands: S-A, Simultaneous and Age.

Decode: Simultaneous exam in India and a higher Age limit: demanded by the WEMC from 1860; first exam in India only in 1922.

Why it sticks: two S-A demands, both refused for decades.

Memory hook

Ripon 80-84 raises, Lytton 76-80 lowers: but the law says SoS.

Decode: guidebooks credit Lytton 76-80 for reducing the age limit and Ripon 80-84 for raising it; the legal truth is that the Secretary of State for India held the authority after the GOI Act 1858.

Why it sticks: a see-saw with the Secretary of State holding the seat.

Handout only (no class notes): Lecture 20 stops at slide 40, so everything from here to the end of the chapter comes from the handout alone

Memory hook

86 appoints, 87 reports, 92 executes: D-D, L-L.

Decode: Dufferin 84-88 appointed the Aitchison Commission 1886; report 1887; implemented 1892 by executive order under Lansdowne 88-94: CCS to ICS, UCS to PCS (plus Subordinate), Statutory CS abolished. Not the Indian Councils Act 1892.

Why it sticks: the two D's and two L's: Dufferin 84-88 appoints, Lansdowne 88-94 lands it.

Memory hook

Islington: 12 sets up, 17 reports; 25% yes, simultaneous exam no.

Decode: 1912 set up, 1917 report; 25% Indians in senior posts (by exam and by promotion from the PCS); rejected a simultaneous exam in India.

Why it sticks: a quarter in, the door to the Indian exam still shut.

Memory hook

19 promises, 22 exam, 26 PSC, 33% posts.

Decode: GOI Act 1919: simultaneous exam in India (first held 1922), Public Service Commission (established 1926), 33% Indians in senior posts; ICS split into AIS and Central services because of Dyarchy.

Why it sticks: 19, 22, 26, 33: the years grow by 3, 4, then the percentage.

Memory hook

Lee: 20-40-40, then 50:50 in 15 years.

Decode: Lee Commission 1923 (report 1924): 20% by promotion from the PCS, 40% Indians by exam, 40% Europeans by exam (total 100); 50:50 in 15 years via exam; set up the PSC already promised in 1919.

Why it sticks: 20+40+40 fills the hundred, and the exam half then moves to equal.

Memory hook

378 transition, 315-316 commissions, 312 services.

Decode: Article 378 moved the Federal PSC (set up 1937, GOI Act 1935) into the UPSC on 26 January 1950; Articles 315 and 316 govern the UPSC and State PSCs; Article 312 governs All India Services. By 1947 more than 50% of posts were Indianised.

Why it sticks: 3-1-8 is the transition, 3-1-5 and 3-1-6 are the commissions, 3-1-2 the services: all start with 31.

4.1 The inherited system (pre-1781)

Memory hook

N-F-K-Z: top to bottom.

Decode: Naib Nazim (head), Faujdar (rural Sarkars), Kotwal (towns), Zamindar (villages, pays the Village Watchmen).

Why it sticks: four letters, four levels, from province down to village.

4.3 The Daroga system of Cornwallis 86-93 (1793)

Memory hook

30 square miles per thana, one Daroga, one DM on top: 93.

Decode: 1793 Cornwallis 86-93: zamindars lose police powers; each district is cut into thanas of 30 square miles, each under an Indian Daroga appointed by the DM; the DM is supreme in the district; chain Daroga, DM, GG and Council. Failure: Daroga-Zamindar nexus, Wellesley 98-05 reverses in 1799, Madras tries it 1802, Torture Commission 1855.

Why it sticks: the thana is the unit, the Daroga is the man, the DM is the boss.

Memory hook

DC collects, DM controls: Collector's two hats.

Decode: DC = revenue (descendant of the Mughal Diwani / Amalguzar); DM = law and order (descendant of the Nizamat / Faujdari), with the SP, issues orders such as Section 144 of the CrPC; the judge's hat was taken off by Cornwallis 86-93 in 1793.

Why it sticks: C for Collect, M for Maintain order; the third hat (judge) is gone.

4.4 The Police Act 1861

Memory hook

Sindh 43, Punjab 49, Bombay 53, Madras 59: S-P-B-M.

Decode: the Irish Constabulary model was road-tested in Sindh (1843, Charles Napier), Punjab (1849), Bombay (1853), Madras (1859); the 1st Police Commission 1860 (Canning 56-62, M.H. Court) copied Sindh; Napier did not head it.

Why it sticks: the years climb 43, 49, 53, 59; "Court" in the name, Napier "gave the plan".

Memory hook

IG over SP over SI: Province, District, Thana.

Decode: IG heads the province (reports to Governor / LG); SP heads the district (reports to DC/DM); SI heads the thana (reports to SP, not the DC). Police Act March 1861; the Indian Councils Act 1861 is August 1861.

Why it sticks: I-S-S climbs down the map from big to small.

Memory hook

93 exam, 03 Fraser, 20 Indians in, 48 IPS, 51 Act.

Decode: 1893 first police exam in London (probationary ASPs); 1902-03 Fraser (Curzon 99-05): Imperial Police, PPS and DySP, CID, DCI 1903, IP closed to Indians; 1920 IP opened (exams in both countries from 1921); 1931 20% SPs Indian; 1939 more Indian SPs (WWII); 1948 IPS by Home Ministry resolution; 1951 All India Services Act.

Why it sticks: "1861 structure, 1902 service, 1948 IPS" is the three-line summary.

5 · The three-pillar administrative legacy (handout only, no class notes)

Memory hook

Crisis or pressure: the British only moved when pushed.

Decode: reforms came after the Great Bengal Famine 1770 or the 1857 revolt (crisis), or as a concession to the Indian National Movement (Indianisation: 1919, 1923, 1935); never beyond what cost or politics demanded. What was left: codified law, organised civil service, hierarchical police.

Why it sticks: two triggers (crisis, pressure), three legacies (law, service, police).

Memory hooks: chapter in one breath

Memory hook

The story of "Judge, Collector, Constable".

Reza Khan wears two hats under the Dual Government (65-72) and the famine of 70 breaks law and order, so Hastings 73-85 takes over in 72: DC sits as civil judge, DC watches the Kazi; a Supreme Court for Britons (74).

Then Cornwallis 86-93 splits the DC from the judge (93) because the new landlords need a fair court: four divisions (P-M-C-D), Circuit like a wheel, Intent not Weapon, Indians out and Lawyers in; he also fathers the CCS and UCS (Group A and B) with pay up, trade down, seniority yes, merit exam not yet.

Bentinck 28-35 bins the divisional courts and Persian (35); Macaulay's codes (59-60-61) and High Courts (62) tidy the law; the Federal Court (37) points to the Supreme Court (50).

The exam opens in 53-55 but with three locks (London, Literature, low age); Indians demand S and A; Lytton 76-80 offers loyalists a lateral door, Aitchison 87 and Lansdowne 88-94 rename (92), 1919 brings the exam to India (22), Lee says 20-40-40, 1935 gives the FPSC (37) and Article 378 the UPSC (50).

The police: N-F-K-Z becomes DM plus Daroga (93), fails in the Daroga-Zamindar nexus; Sindh 43 becomes the Police Act 1861 (IG, SP, SI); Fraser 1902-03 builds the service; 1948-51 the IPS.

Decode: 1765-72, 1772, 1774, 1793, 1835, 1859-62, 1853-55, 1892, 1919-22, 1923-24, 1935-37, 1950, 1861, 1902-03, 1948-51.
